   307	def normalize_candidate(candidate: dict, source_type: str, run_date: str, ordinal: int) -> dict:
   308	    urls = candidate.get("urls") or []
   309	    primary_url = urls[0] if urls else ""
   310	    source_name = candidate.get("source_name", "Unknown")
   311	    headline = candidate.get("subject") or candidate.get("snippet") or "(untitled)"
   312	    candidate_id = article_id_for(
   313	        primary_url or f"{source_type}:{source_name}:{headline}:{ordinal}",
   314	        source_name,
   315	    )
   316	    return {
   317	        "candidate_id": candidate_id,
   318	        "run_date": run_date,
   319	        "source_type": source_type,
   320	        "source_name": source_name,
   321	        "headline": headline,
   322	        "snippet": candidate.get("snippet", ""),
   323	        "primary_url": primary_url,
   324	        "urls": urls[:5],
   325	        "category": candidate.get("category", "unknown"),
   326	        "priority": candidate.get("priority", "normal"),
   327	        "tier": candidate.get("tier", 0),
   328	        "score": candidate.get("score"),
   329	        "sender_email": candidate.get("sender_email"),
   330	        "sender": candidate.get("sender"),
   331	        "published_at": candidate.get("date"),
   332	        # Journal TOC emails carry their own body text + anchor-text/url pairs
   333	        # (see gmail_reader) — empty for every other source.
   334	        "body_excerpt": candidate.get("body_excerpt", ""),
   335	        "link_titles": candidate.get("link_titles", []),
   336	    }
   337	
   338	
   339	def extract_candidate_signals(candidate: dict, ticker_lookup: set[str],
   340	                              company_lookup: dict[str, str] | None = None,
   341	                              ticker_details: dict[str, dict] | None = None) -> list[str]:
   342	    text = " ".join(
   343	        str(candidate.get(field, ""))
   344	        for field in ("headline", "snippet", "source_name", "category")
   345	    )
   346	    # Match ticker symbols
   347	    tokens = set(re.findall(r"\b[A-Z]{2,6}\b", text.upper()))
   348	    ticker_hits = sorted(token for token in tokens if token in ticker_lookup)[:3]
   349	
   350	    # Match company names in headlines
   351	    if company_lookup:
   352	        text_lower = text.lower()
   353	        for name, ticker in company_lookup.items():
   354	            if len(name) >= 5 and name in text_lower:
   355	                base = ticker.split(".")[0].upper()
   356	                if base not in ticker_hits:
   357	                    ticker_hits.append(base)
   358	                    if len(ticker_hits) >= 5:
   359	                        break
   360	
   361	    signals = []
   362	    if candidate.get("priority") == "high":
   363	        signals.append("priority:high")
   364	    if candidate.get("source_type") == "gmail":
   365	        signals.append("source_type:gmail")
   366	    if candidate.get("source_type") == "tier2":
   367	        signals.append("source_type:tier2")
   368	    if candidate.get("score"):
   369	        signals.append(f"hn_score:{candidate['score']}")
   370	    if candidate.get("category"):
   371	        signals.append(f"category:{candidate['category']}")
   372	    signals.extend(f"ticker:{ticker}" for ticker in ticker_hits)
   373	
   374	    # Add subsector tags from matched tickers
   375	    if ticker_details and ticker_hits:
   376	        subsectors_seen = set()
   377	        for ticker in ticker_hits:
   378	            detail = ticker_details.get(ticker) or ticker_details.get(ticker.upper())
   379	            if detail and detail.get("subsector") and detail["subsector"] not in subsectors_seen:
   380	                signals.append(f"subsector:{detail['subsector']}")
   381	                subsectors_seen.add(detail["subsector"])
   382	
   383	    return signals
   384	
   385	
   386	def build_structured_candidates(
   387	    gmail_items: list[dict],
   388	    tier2_items: list[dict],
   389	    run_date: str,
   390	    tickers: dict,
   391	) -> tuple[list[dict], list[dict]]:
   392	    ticker_lookup = {
   393	        str(ticker).upper()
   394	        for bucket in ("healthcare", "tech", "other")
   395	        for ticker in tickers.get(bucket, [])
   396	        if isinstance(ticker, str)
   397	    }
   398	    company_lookup = tickers.get("company_lookup") or {}
   399	    ticker_details = tickers.get("details") or {}
   400	
   401	    normalized_gmail = [
   402	        normalize_candidate(item, "gmail", run_date, index)
   403	        for index, item in enumerate(gmail_items, 1)
   404	    ]
   405	    normalized_tier2 = [
   406	        normalize_candidate(item, "tier2", run_date, index)
   407	        for index, item in enumerate(tier2_items, 1)
   408	    ]
   409	
   410	    # Dedupe by candidate_id. Gmail delivers copies of the same newsletter
   411	    # to multiple plus-aliases (e.g. +finance) as separate messages, so a
   412	    # single article can produce two normalized candidates with identical
   413	    # IDs but different Gmail-message metadata (date, message_id). Keep
   414	    # the first occurrence — Gmail returns most-recent first.
   415	    def _dedupe(candidates: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
   416	        seen: set[tuple[str, str]] = set()
   417	        out: list[dict] = []
   418	        for c in candidates:
   419	            cid = c.get("candidate_id")
   420	            if not cid:
   421	                out.append(c)
   422	                continue
   423	            # Key on (candidate_id, headline), not candidate_id alone. The
   424	            # candidate_id hashes only the first URL + source, so two distinct
   425	            # newsletter editions that share a first link (a common sponsor/ad
   426	            # URL, e.g. the same webinar promo atop consecutive Fierce issues)
   427	            # collapse to one id — deduping on id alone silently drops the
   428	            # later, genuinely-distinct edition. Plus-alias copies (the case
   429	            # this dedupe targets) share both id AND headline, so they still
   430	            # collapse correctly.
   431	            key = (cid, (c.get("headline") or "").casefold())
   432	            if key in seen:
   433	                continue
   434	            seen.add(key)
   435	            out.append(c)
   436	        return out
   437	
   438	    before = len(normalized_gmail) + len(normalized_tier2)
   439	    normalized_gmail = _dedupe(normalized_gmail)
   440	    normalized_tier2 = _dedupe(normalized_tier2)
   441	    dropped = before - len(normalized_gmail) - len(normalized_tier2)
   442	    if dropped:
   443	        print(f"  Deduped {dropped} duplicate candidate(s) by candidate_id")
   444	
   445	    for candidate in normalized_gmail + normalized_tier2:
   446	        candidate["derived_signals"] = extract_candidate_signals(
   447	            candidate, ticker_lookup, company_lookup, ticker_details,
   448	        )
   449	
   450	    return normalized_gmail, normalized_tier2
   451	
   452	
   453	def score_candidate_for_triage(candidate: dict) -> int:
   454	    score = 0
   455	    if candidate.get("source_type") == "gmail":
   456	        score += 3
   457	    if candidate.get("priority") == "high":
   458	        score += 2
   459	    if candidate.get("tier") == 1:
   460	        score += 2
   461	    score += len([signal for signal in candidate.get("derived_signals", []) if signal.startswith("ticker:")]) * 2
   462	    if candidate.get("score"):
   463	        score += min(int(candidate["score"]) // 50, 3)
   464	    return score
   465	
   466	
   467	INTEREST_BUCKETS = {
   468	    "healthcare": {"healthcare_daily", "healthcare_weekly", "healthcare_policy"},
   469	    "finance_macro": {"finance_macro"},
   470	    "tech_ai": {"tech_ai"},
   471	    "broad_curious": {"broad_curious"},
   472	}
   473	
   474	
   475	def _category_to_bucket(category: str) -> str:
   476	    for bucket, categories in INTEREST_BUCKETS.items():
   477	        if category in categories:
   478	            return bucket
   479	    return "other"
   480	
   481	
   482	def build_triage_queue(
   483	    structured_gmail: list[dict],
   484	    structured_tier2: list[dict],
   485	    selected_articles: list[dict],
   486	    limit: int = 10,
   487	) -> list[dict]:
   488	    selected_urls = {article.get("url") for article in selected_articles}
   489	    always_read_names = get_always_read_names()
   490	    journal_names = get_journal_source_names()
   491	    scored = []
   492	    for candidate in structured_gmail + structured_tier2:
   493	        if candidate.get("primary_url") in selected_urls:
   494	            continue
   495	        if candidate.get("source_name", "") in always_read_names:
   496	            continue
   497	        # Journal issue emails get their own digest section (build_journal_watch)
   498	        # — a raw TOC link in "Also considered" would be noise.
   499	        if candidate.get("source_name", "") in journal_names:
   500	            continue
   501	        # Skip candidates whose links were dropped as dead-end redirectors
   502	        # (e.g. McKinsey/Atlantic tokens that resolve to publisher homepage
   503	        # or ad-tracker). A "# → nowhere" link in the digest is worse than
   504	        # omitting the candidate.
   505	        if not candidate.get("primary_url"):
   506	            continue
   507	        scored.append({
   508	            **candidate,
   509	            "triage_score": score_candidate_for_triage(candidate),
   510	        })
   511	
   512	    scored.sort(
   513	        key=lambda candidate: (
   514	            -candidate.get("triage_score", 0),
   515	            candidate.get("source_name", ""),
   516	            candidate.get("headline", ""),
   517	        )
   518	    )
   519	
   520	    # Ensure at least one candidate from each major interest bucket
   521	    queue = []
   522	    buckets_covered = set()
   523	    # First pass: pick the top candidate per bucket
   524	    for candidate in scored:
   525	        bucket = _category_to_bucket(candidate.get("category", "unknown"))
   526	        if bucket not in buckets_covered:
   527	            queue.append(candidate)
   528	            buckets_covered.add(bucket)
   529	    # Second pass: fill remaining slots by score
   530	    for candidate in scored:
   531	        if len(queue) >= limit:
   532	            break
   533	        if candidate not in queue:
   534	            queue.append(candidate)
   535	
   536	    return queue[:limit]
   537	
   538	
   539	def build_always_read(
   540	    structured_gmail: list[dict],
   541	    selected_articles: list[dict],
   542	) -> list[dict]:
   543	    """Extract candidates from always-read paid sources."""
   544	    always_read_names = get_always_read_names()
   545	    if not always_read_names:
   546	        return []
   547	    selected_urls = {a.get("url") for a in selected_articles}
   548	    results = []
   549	    for candidate in structured_gmail:
   550	        source_name = candidate.get("source_name", "")
   551	        if source_name not in always_read_names:
   552	            continue
   553	        if candidate.get("primary_url") in selected_urls:
   554	            continue
   555	        # Drop URL-less candidates (dead-end redirector) — see build_triage_queue.
   556	        if not candidate.get("primary_url"):
   557	            continue
   558	        results.append(candidate)
   559	    return results
   560	
   561	
   562	def _journal_picks_from_toc(issue: dict, toc_text: str) -> list[dict]:
   563	    """Ask Claude to nominate 0-2 must-read articles from a journal issue TOC.
   564	
   565	    Returns [] on any failure (missing key, refusal, bad JSON) — the caller
   566	    degrades to listing the issue link, never dropping the section silently.
   567	    """
   568	    today = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
   569	    try:
   570	        client = anthropic.Anthropic()
   571	        resp = client.messages.create(
   572	            model="claude-sonnet-4-6",
   573	            max_tokens=800,
   574	            messages=[{
   575	                "role": "user",
   576	                "content": (
   577	                    f"Today is {today}. Below is the table of contents of a medical/"
   578	                    f"health-policy journal issue ({issue.get('source_name', '')} — "
   579	                    f"{issue.get('headline', '')}).\n\n"
   580	                    "The reader is a healthcare-focused public-equity investor: he cares "
   581	                    "about clinical results that move drug/device markets (obesity/GLP-1, "
   582	                    "oncology, cardiology, gene therapy), healthcare policy & payment "
   583	                    "(CMS, Medicare Advantage, drug pricing), health-system economics, "
   584	                    "and durable scientific insight. He does NOT need clinical-practice "
   585	                    "minutiae, case images, or narrow specialty technique papers.\n\n"
   586	                    "Pick the 0-2 articles from this issue MOST worth his time. Fewer is "
   587	                    "better — pick zero if nothing clears the bar. Reply with ONLY a JSON "
   588	                    "array (no prose): "
   589	                    '[{"title": "...", "url": "...", "why": "one sentence"}] '
   590	                    "(url empty string if not visible in the TOC text).\n\n"
   591	                    f"TOC:\n{toc_text[:6000]}"
   592	                ),
   593	            }],
   594	        )
   595	        picks = _extract_json_array(resp.content)
   596	        clean = []
   597	        for p in picks[:2]:
   598	            if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("title"):
   599	                clean.append({
   600	                    "title": str(p.get("title"))[:200],
   601	                    "url": str(p.get("url") or "")[:500],
   602	                    "why": str(p.get("why") or "")[:300],
   603	                })
   604	        return clean
   605	    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — non-gating by design
   606	        print(f"  [journals] pick failed for {issue.get('headline', '')!r}: {exc}")
   607	        return []
   608	
   609	
   610	def _resolve_pick_urls(picks: list[dict], link_titles: list[dict]) -> None:
   611	    """Fill each pick's url from the issue email's anchor-text->href pairs.
   612	
   613	    The TOC text Claude reads is tag-stripped (inlining ~400-char tracking URLs
   614	    would blow the prompt budget), so picked titles come back URL-less (codex
   615	    2026-07-08). Match by containment either way after lowercasing; leave the
   616	    url empty when nothing matches (the Slack render already handles that)."""
   617	    if not link_titles:
   618	        return
   619	    pairs = [(str(lt.get("text", "")).lower(), str(lt.get("url", "")))
   620	             for lt in link_titles if isinstance(lt, dict) and lt.get("url")]
   621	    for pick in picks:
   622	        if pick.get("url"):
   623	            continue
   624	        title = str(pick.get("title", "")).lower().strip()
   625	        if len(title) < 10:
   626	            continue
   627	        for text, url in pairs:
   628	            if title in text or text in title:
   629	                pick["url"] = url
   630	                break
   631	
   632	
   633	def build_journal_watch(structured_gmail: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
   634	    """Journals section (JP 2026-07-06): each journal-category email is an
   635	    ISSUE (NEJM TOC, Weekend Briefing; Health Affairs once subscribed).
   636	    For each issue, extract the TOC page and have Claude flag the 1-2
   637	    articles worth reading. Failures degrade to a bare issue link.
   638	    """
   639	    journal_names = get_journal_source_names()
   640	    if not journal_names:
   641	        return []
   642	    issues = [c for c in structured_gmail if c.get("source_name") in journal_names]
   643	    if not issues:
   644	        return []
   645	    section("JOURNAL WATCH")
   646	    out = []
   647	    for issue in issues:
   648	        toc_url = issue.get("primary_url") or ""
   649	        picks: list[dict] = []
   650	        # The email body IS the TOC (nejm.org blocks scraping, so the web
   651	        # fetch is only a fallback for e.g. re-processed artifacts that
   652	        # predate body_excerpt).
   653	        toc_text = issue.get("body_excerpt") or ""
   654	        tier = "email_body"
   655	        if not toc_text and toc_url:
   656	            toc_text, tier = fetch_article_text(toc_url, timeout=20)
   657	        if toc_text:
   658	            picks = _journal_picks_from_toc(issue, toc_text)
   659	            _resolve_pick_urls(picks, issue.get("link_titles") or [])
   660	            print(f"  {issue.get('source_name')}: {len(picks)} pick(s) "
   661	                  f"(TOC via {tier})")
   662	        else:
   663	            print(f"  {issue.get('source_name')}: no TOC text — "
   664	                  f"listing issue link only")
   665	        out.append({
   666	            "source_name": issue.get("source_name", ""),
   667	            "headline": issue.get("headline", "(untitled issue)"),
   668	            "url": toc_url,
   669	            "picks": picks,
   670	        })
   671	    return out
   672	
   673	
   674	def _dropped_detail(surfaces) -> list[dict]:
   675	    """Name every item a liveness probe is about to drop.
   676	
   677	    `surfaces` is `[(items, dropped_indices, surface_label), ...]`, read BEFORE
   678	    the lists are filtered — afterwards the indices address different items.
   679	
   680	    The three drop buckets (`always_read`, `triage`, `substack`) recorded only
   681	    a COUNT. Measured on the stored log, `always_read_dropped: 1` appears on 10
   682	    of the 37 runs since 2026-07-01 and every one is anonymous, so the "6 broken
   683	    always-read URLs" on board row #261 could be counted and never fixed. An
   684	    out-of-range index is skipped rather than raised on: this runs inside
   685	    delivery, and a logging detail must never be able to take the digest down.
   686	    """
   687	    out = []
   688	    for items, dropped, surface in surfaces:
   689	        for idx in sorted(dropped or ()):
   690	            if not (0 <= idx < len(items)):
   691	                continue
   692	            item = items[idx] or {}
   693	            out.append({
   694	                "surface": surface,
   695	                "source": item.get("source") or item.get("source_name") or "",
   696	                "headline": (item.get("headline") or item.get("title") or "")[:120],
   697	                "url": item.get("url") or "",
   698	            })
   699	    return out
   700	
   701	
   702	def validate_delivery_urls(
   703	    articles: list[dict],
   704	    triage_queue: list[dict],
   705	    always_read: list[dict],
   706	    substack_items: list[dict],
   707	    exclude_ids: set[str] | None = None,
   708	) -> tuple[list[dict], list[dict], list[dict], list[dict]]:
   709	    """Probe every URL that is about to ship. Drops broken URLs from
   710	    triage/always_read/substack lists. For main-slot articles (which are
   711	    load-bearing — dropping one would leave an empty slot), logs a loud
   712	    warning but keeps the article so the digest still goes out.
   713	
   714	    Uses `url_resolver.check_urls_live` which treats 404/410 and
   715	    connection errors as broken but keeps 401/403/timeouts (paywalled or
   716	    bot-walled sites that work fine in the user's browser).
   717	    """
   718	    section("DELIVERY URL VALIDATION")
   719	
   720	    # Gather every URL we're about to ship, with its source surface.
   721	    # (surface, index, url)
   722	    urls_to_check: list[tuple[str, int, str]] = []
   723	    for i, a in enumerate(articles):
   724	        url = (a.get("url") or "").strip()
   725	        if url:
   726	            urls_to_check.append(("article", i, url))
   727	    for i, c in enumerate(triage_queue):
   728	        url = (c.get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   729	        if url:
   730	            urls_to_check.append(("triage", i, url))
   731	    for i, c in enumerate(always_read):
   732	        url = (c.get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   733	        if url:
   734	            urls_to_check.append(("always_read", i, url))
   735	    for i, item in enumerate(substack_items):
   736	        url = (item.get("url") or "").strip()
   737	        if url:
   738	            urls_to_check.append(("substack", i, url))
   739	
   740	    unique_urls = list({url for _, _, url in urls_to_check})
   741	    if not unique_urls:
   742	        print("No URLs to validate.")
   743	        return articles, triage_queue, always_read, substack_items
   744	
   745	    print(f"Probing {len(unique_urls)} unique URLs across {len(urls_to_check)} slots...")
   746	    liveness = check_urls_live(unique_urls, timeout=3)
   747	
   748	    broken_articles: list[int] = []
   749	    broken_triage: set[int] = set()
   750	    broken_always_read: set[int] = set()
   751	    broken_substack: set[int] = set()
   752	    for surface, idx, url in urls_to_check:
   753	        if liveness.get(url, True):
   754	            continue
   755	        if surface == "article":
   756	            broken_articles.append(idx)
   757	        elif surface == "triage":
   758	            broken_triage.add(idx)
   759	        elif surface == "always_read":
   760	            broken_always_read.add(idx)
   761	        elif surface == "substack":
   762	            broken_substack.add(idx)
   763	
   764	    # A broken main-slot URL used to ship as-is with a warning, because dropping
   765	    # it would leave an empty slot. But a dead link in the top slot is a
   766	    # reader-visible failure on a run that still reports `ok` (JP, 2026-07-19).
   767	    # So: promote the best still-live "also considered" candidate into the slot
   768	    # instead. Shipping broken is now only the last resort when no eligible
   769	    # substitute exists — never a silent degradation either way.
   770	    promoted_triage: set[int] = set()
   771	    substituted: list[dict] = []
   772	    still_broken: list[int] = []
   773	
   774	    # Sources already spoken for, so a substitute can't duplicate one (the same
   775	    # constraint validate_selected_articles enforces at selection time).
   776	    taken_sources = {
   777	        str(a.get("source", "")).strip().casefold()
   778	        for i, a in enumerate(articles)
   779	        if i not in broken_articles and str(a.get("source", "")).strip()
   780	    }
   781	    taken_urls = {
   782	        (a.get("url") or "").strip()
   783	        for i, a in enumerate(articles) if i not in broken_articles
   784	    }
   785	
   786	    # Best-first pool: live-URL triage candidates, highest triage_score first.
   787	    # build_triage_queue reorders for bucket coverage, so re-sort by score here.
   788	    #
   789	    # `exclude_ids` is load-bearing (codex 2026-07-20): select_articles() filters
   790	    # recently-delivered candidates, but build_triage_queue() does NOT — it is
   791	    # rebuilt from the full candidate set. Without this check a candidate that
   792	    # the cross-run dedupe deliberately kept out of today's selection could be
   793	    # promoted straight into a main slot and re-delivered.
   794	    _excluded = exclude_ids or set()
   795	    pool = sorted(
   796	        (
   797	            i for i in range(len(triage_queue))
   798	            if i not in broken_triage
   799	            and (triage_queue[i].get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   800	            and triage_queue[i].get("candidate_id") not in _excluded
   801	        ),
   802	        key=lambda i: -int(triage_queue[i].get("triage_score") or 0),
   803	    )
   804	
   805	    for idx in sorted(broken_articles, key=lambda i: articles[i].get("slot", 0)):
   806	        a = articles[idx]
   807	        slot = a.get("slot", "?")
   808	        sub_i = None
   809	        for i in pool:
   810	            if i in promoted_triage:
   811	                continue
   812	            c = triage_queue[i]
   813	            src = str(c.get("source_name", "")).strip()
   814	            url = (c.get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   815	            if not src or not url:
   816	                continue
   817	            if src.casefold() in taken_sources or url in taken_urls:
   818	                continue
   819	            sub_i = i
   820	            break
   821	
   822	        if sub_i is None:
   823	            still_broken.append(idx)
   824	            print(
   825	                f"  WARNING: main slot {slot} URL is broken and NO eligible "
   826	                f"substitute was available — shipping broken to preserve the "
   827	                f"slot. Headline: {a.get('headline', '')[:60]}"
   828	            )
   829	            print(f"    URL: {a.get('url', '')}")
   830	            continue
   831	
   832	        c = triage_queue[sub_i]
   833	        src = str(c.get("source_name", "")).strip()
   834	        url = (c.get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   835	        # Triage candidates carry no editorial summary/why-it-matters (those are
   836	        # written by the selection LLM). Rather than spend a second LLM call on
   837	        # a fallback path, use the candidate's own snippet and say plainly in
   838	        # the digest that this is a substitution — honest beats fabricated.
   839	        snippet = str(c.get("snippet") or c.get("body_excerpt") or "").strip()
   840	        snippet = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", snippet)[:400]
   841	        articles[idx] = {
   842	            "article_id": article_id_for(url, src),
   843	            "headline": str(c.get("headline") or "(untitled)").strip(),
   844	            "source": src,
   845	            "url": url,
   846	            "slot": a.get("slot"),
   847	            "summary": snippet or "(no summary available — promoted from the "
   848	                                  "also-considered queue)",
   849	            "why_it_matters": (
   850	                f"Promoted from 'also considered' because the original slot-{slot} "
   851	                f"pick ({a.get('source', 'unknown source')}) shipped a dead link."
   852	            ),
   853	            "signal_tags": [],
   854	            "reading_time": "N/A",
   855	        }
   856	        promoted_triage.add(sub_i)
   857	        taken_sources.add(src.casefold())
   858	        taken_urls.add(url)
   859	        substituted.append({
   860	            "slot": a.get("slot"),
   861	            "broken_source": a.get("source", ""),
   862	            "broken_url": a.get("url", ""),
   863	            "substitute_source": src,
   864	            "substitute_headline": str(c.get("headline") or "")[:120],
   865	            "substitute_url": url,
   866	        })
   867	        print(
   868	            f"  SUBSTITUTED main slot {slot}: broken link from "
   869	            f"{a.get('source', '?')} replaced with {src} — "
   870	            f"{str(c.get('headline') or '')[:60]}"
   871	        )
   872	
   873	    def _filter(
   874	        items: list[dict],
   875	        dropped: set[int],
   876	        label: str,
   877	        silent: set[int] | None = None,
   878	    ) -> list[dict]:
   879	        """Remove `dropped` indices. Indices in `silent` are removed without a
   880	        "URL failed liveness probe" line — used for promoted candidates, which
   881	        leave the queue for a different (non-failure) reason."""
   882	        if not dropped:
   883	            return items
   884	        skip_log = silent or set()
   885	        for idx in sorted(dropped):
   886	            if idx in skip_log:
   887	                continue
   888	            c = items[idx]
   889	            headline = c.get("headline") or c.get("subject", "Untitled")
   890	            print(f"  Dropping {label}: {headline[:60]} — URL failed liveness probe")
   891	        return [c for i, c in enumerate(items) if i not in dropped]
   892	
   893	    # Promoted candidates leave the triage queue so they don't appear twice in
   894	    # the digest (once in a main slot, once under "also considered").
   895	    for i in sorted(promoted_triage):
   896	        print(
   897	            f"  Removing from triage (promoted to a main slot): "
   898	            f"{str(triage_queue[i].get('headline') or '')[:60]}"
   899	        )
   900	    # Capture WHICH items are about to be dropped, before `_filter` reindexes
   901	    # the lists — afterwards the indices no longer address the same items.
   902	    dropped_detail = _dropped_detail([
   903	        (always_read, broken_always_read, "always_read"),
   904	        (triage_queue, broken_triage - promoted_triage, "triage"),
   905	        (substack_items, broken_substack, "substack"),

   907

   912	    substack_items = _filter(substack_items, broken_substack, "substack")

   915	        len(broken_articles) + len(broken_triage)

   925	            f"{len(broken_triage)} triage dropped, "
   926	            f"{len(broken_always_read)} always-read dropped, "
   927	            f"{len(broken_substack)} substack dropped)."

   930	    # Append this run's validation counts to the log so the Friday weekly
   931	    # report can surface URL-health trends.

   975	def validate_selected_articles(articles: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
   976	    """Enforce structural rules before delivering a digest."""
   977	    required_slots = {1, 2, 3}
   978	    allowed_slots = {1, 2, 3, 4}
   979	    required_fields = ("headline", "source", "url", "slot", "summary", "why_it_matters")
   980	
   981	    validated = []
   982	    seen_slots = set()
   983	    seen_sources = set()
   984	
   985	    for index, raw_article in enumerate(articles, 1):
   986	        if not isinstance(raw_article, dict):
   987	            print(f"  Rejecting article #{index}: not an object")
   988	            continue
   989	
   990	        article = {key: raw_article.get(key) for key in required_fields}
   991	        missing = [field for field, value in article.items() if value in (None, "", [])]
   992	        if missing:
   993	            print(f"  Rejecting article #{index}: missing {', '.join(missing)}")
   994	            continue
   995	
   996	        try:
   997	            slot = int(article["slot"])
   998	        except (TypeError, ValueError):
   999	            print(f"  Rejecting article #{index}: invalid slot {article['slot']!r}")
  1000	            continue
  1001	
  1002	        if slot not in allowed_slots:
  1003	            print(f"  Rejecting article #{index}: slot {slot} is out of range")
  1004	            continue
  1005	        if slot in seen_slots:
  1006	            print(f"  Rejecting article #{index}: duplicate slot {slot}")
  1007	            continue
  1008	
  1009	        source = str(article["source"]).strip()
  1010	        normalized_source = source.casefold()
  1011	        if normalized_source in seen_sources:
  1012	            print(f"  Rejecting article #{index}: duplicate source {source}")
  1013	            continue
  1014	
  1015	        url = str(article["url"]).strip()
  1016	        if not re.match(r"^https?://", url):
  1017	            print(f"  Rejecting article #{index}: invalid URL {url!r}")
  1018	            continue
  1019	
  1020	        signal_tags = raw_article.get("signal_tags", [])
  1021	        if not isinstance(signal_tags, list):
  1022	            signal_tags = [str(signal_tags)]
  1023	
  1024	        validated.append({
  1025	            "article_id": article_id_for(url, source),
  1026	            "headline": str(article["headline"]).strip(),
  1027	            "source": source,
  1028	            "url": url,
  1029	            "slot": slot,
  1030	            "summary": str(article["summary"]).strip(),
  1031	            "why_it_matters": str(article["why_it_matters"]).strip(),
  1032	            "signal_tags": [str(tag).strip() for tag in signal_tags if str(tag).strip()],
  1033	            "reading_time": str(raw_article.get("reading_time", "N/A")).strip() or "N/A",
  1034	        })
  1035	        seen_slots.add(slot)
  1036	        seen_sources.add(normalized_source)
  1037	
  1038	        if len(validated) == 4:
  1039	            break
  1040	
  1041	    present_slots = {article["slot"] for article in validated}
  1042	    missing_required_slots = sorted(required_slots - present_slots)
  1043	    if missing_required_slots:
  1044	        print(
  1045	            "Validation failed: missing required slot(s): "
  1046	            + ", ".join(str(slot) for slot in missing_required_slots)
  1047	        )
  1048	        return []
  1049	
  1050	    return sorted(validated, key=lambda article: article["slot"])
  1051

  1053	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1054	# [GMAIL SCAN]
  1055	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1056	
  1057	# Canary sources: senders that reliably email every weekday. If a 7-day
  1058	# gmail scan returns zero from any of these, something is broken —
  1059	# pipeline bug, OAuth expiry, Gmail query malformed, or unchecked
  1060	# address drift. Fail loudly rather than silently proceed.
  1061	# Kept deliberately small; expanding this list means more false-positive
  1062	# risk from legitimate publishing gaps (holidays, sender outages).
  1063	CANARY_SOURCES = ("Fierce Biotech", "Fierce Pharma", "BioSpace")
  1064	
  1065	
  1066	def gmail_scan() -> list[dict]:
  1067	    section("GMAIL SCAN")
  1068	    try:
  1069	        # 7-day rolling window makes ingestion self-healing: if a sender
  1070	        # address is corrected or a workflow run is skipped, subsequent
  1071	        # runs backfill the missed emails. Safe because
  1072	        # build_structured_candidates dedupes by candidate_id.
  1073	        items = fetch_newsletters(hours_back=168)
  1074	        print(f"Found {len(items)} newsletter emails")
  1075	        sources_found = set(i["source_name"] for i in items)
  1076	        for s in sorted(sources_found):
  1077	            count = sum(1 for i in items if i["source_name"] == s)
  1078	            print(f"  - {s}: {count} email(s)")
  1079	
  1080	        # Canary check — if every canary is silent across a 7-day window,
  1081	        # something is systematically wrong (Apr 12's all-zero gmail scan
  1082	        # would have been caught here). Raises to trigger Slack alert from
  1083	        # the workflow's failure path + stops the digest before it ships
  1084	        # noise.
  1085	        silent = [c for c in CANARY_SOURCES if c not in sources_found]
  1086	        if len(silent) == len(CANARY_SOURCES):
  1087	            raise RuntimeError(
  1088	                f"Canary failure: zero emails from any of {CANARY_SOURCES} "
  1089	                f"in the 7-day window. Likely pipeline bug, OAuth expiry, "
  1090	                f"or all-sources address drift."
  1091	            )
  1092	        if silent:
  1093	            print(
  1094	                f"  WARNING: canary source(s) silent this week: {silent}. "
  1095	                f"Check if sender address changed or subscription lapsed."
  1096	            )
  1097	
  1098	        return items
  1099	    except RuntimeError:
  1100	        # Canary failure — let it propagate so the workflow fails visibly.
  1101	        raise
  1102	    except Exception as e:
  1103	        print(f"Gmail scan failed: {e}")
  1104	        print("Continuing with Tier 2 sources only...")
  1105	        # NO SILENT FAILURES: a hard Gmail API failure (403/500/timeout/
  1106	        # malformed message) throws before the canary can run, so it would
  1107	        # otherwise degrade to an empty Gmail corpus with a clean "ok"
  1108	        # heartbeat. Record a partial reason so the run is flagged while the
  1109	        # digest still ships from RSS/HN (warn-and-proceed).
  1110	        _RUN_STATE["partial_reasons"].append(
  1111	            f"Gmail scan failed ({type(e).__name__}) — digest built from RSS/HN only"
  1112	        )
  1113	        return []
  1114	
  1115	
  1116	def substack_scan() -> list[dict]:
  1117	    section("SUBSTACK SCAN")
  1118	    try:
  1119	        items = fetch_substack_emails(hours_back=26)
  1120	        # Deduplicate by (sender_email, subject) — Substack occasionally
  1121	        # resends; keep the first we saw.
  1122	        seen: set[tuple[str, str]] = set()
  1123	        unique: list[dict] = []
  1124	        for item in items:
  1125	            key = (item.get("sender_email", ""), item.get("subject", ""))
  1126	            if key in seen:
  1127	                continue
  1128	            seen.add(key)
  1129	            unique.append(item)
  1130	        unique.sort(key=lambda i: i.get("sender_name", "").lower())
  1131	        print(f"Found {len(unique)} Substack email(s) in last 26h")
  1132	        for item in unique:
  1133	            print(f"  - {item.get('sender_name', '')}: {item.get('subject', '')}")
  1134	        return unique
  1135	    except Exception as e:
  1136	        print(f"Substack scan failed: {e}")
  1137	        return []

  1195	        print(f"  RSS scan failed: {e}")
  1196	        _RUN_STATE["partial_reasons"].append(f"RSS scan failed: {e}")

   386	def build_structured_candidates(
   387	    gmail_items: list[dict],

   389	    run_date: str,

   391	) -> tuple[list[dict], list[dict]]:
   392	    ticker_lookup = {
   393	        str(ticker).upper()
   394	        for bucket in ("healthcare", "tech", "other")
   395	        for ticker in tickers.get(bucket, [])
   396	        if isinstance(ticker, str)
   397	    }
   398	    company_lookup = tickers.get("company_lookup") or {}
   399	    ticker_details = tickers.get("details") or {}
   400	
   401	    normalized_gmail = [
   402	        normalize_candidate(item, "gmail", run_date, index)
   403	        for index, item in enumerate(gmail_items, 1)
   404	    ]
   405	    normalized_tier2 = [
   406	        normalize_candidate(item, "tier2", run_date, index)
   407	        for index, item in enumerate(tier2_items, 1)
   408	    ]
   409	
   410	    # Dedupe by candidate_id. Gmail delivers copies of the same newsletter
   411	    # to multiple plus-aliases (e.g. +finance) as separate messages, so a
   412	    # single article can produce two normalized candidates with identical
   413	    # IDs but different Gmail-message metadata (date, message_id). Keep
   414	    # the first occurrence — Gmail returns most-recent first.
   415	    def _dedupe(candidates: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
   416	        seen: set[tuple[str, str]] = set()
   417	        out: list[dict] = []
   418	        for c in candidates:
   419	            cid = c.get("candidate_id")
   420	            if not cid:
   421	                out.append(c)
   422	                continue
   423	            # Key on (candidate_id, headline), not candidate_id alone. The
   424	            # candidate_id hashes only the first URL + source, so two distinct
   425	            # newsletter editions that share a first link (a common sponsor/ad
   426	            # URL, e.g. the same webinar promo atop consecutive Fierce issues)
   427	            # collapse to one id — deduping on id alone silently drops the
   428	            # later, genuinely-distinct edition. Plus-alias copies (the case
   429	            # this dedupe targets) share both id AND headline, so they still
   430	            # collapse correctly.
   431	            key = (cid, (c.get("headline") or "").casefold())
   432	            if key in seen:
   433	                continue
   434	            seen.add(key)
   435	            out.append(c)
   436	        return out
   437	
   438	    before = len(normalized_gmail) + len(normalized_tier2)
   439	    normalized_gmail = _dedupe(normalized_gmail)
   440	    normalized_tier2 = _dedupe(normalized_tier2)
   441	    dropped = before - len(normalized_gmail) - len(normalized_tier2)
   442	    if dropped:
   443	        print(f"  Deduped {dropped} duplicate candidate(s) by candidate_id")
   444	
   445	    for candidate in normalized_gmail + normalized_tier2:
   446	        candidate["derived_signals"] = extract_candidate_signals(
   447	            candidate, ticker_lookup, company_lookup, ticker_details,
   448	        )
   449	
   450	    return normalized_gmail, normalized_tier2

   453	def score_candidate_for_triage(candidate: dict) -> int:
   454	    score = 0
   455	    if candidate.get("source_type") == "gmail":
   456	        score += 3
   457	    if candidate.get("priority") == "high":
   458	        score += 2
   459	    if candidate.get("tier") == 1:
   460	        score += 2
   461	    score += len([signal for signal in candidate.get("derived_signals", []) if signal.startswith("ticker:")]) * 2
   462	    if candidate.get("score"):
   463	        score += min(int(candidate["score"]) // 50, 3)
   464	    return score
   465

   467	INTEREST_BUCKETS = {
   468	    "healthcare": {"healthcare_daily", "healthcare_weekly", "healthcare_policy"},
   469	    "finance_macro": {"finance_macro"},
   470	    "tech_ai": {"tech_ai"},
   471	    "broad_curious": {"broad_curious"},
   472	}
   473	
   474	
   475	def _category_to_bucket(category: str) -> str:
   476	    for bucket, categories in INTEREST_BUCKETS.items():
   477	        if category in categories:
   478	            return bucket
   479	    return "other"

   481	
   482	def build_triage_queue(
   483	    structured_gmail: list[dict],
   484	    structured_tier2: list[dict],
   485	    selected_articles: list[dict],
   486	    limit: int = 10,
   487	) -> list[dict]:
   488	    selected_urls = {article.get("url") for article in selected_articles}
   489	    always_read_names = get_always_read_names()
   490	    journal_names = get_journal_source_names()
   491	    scored = []
   492	    for candidate in structured_gmail + structured_tier2:
   493	        if candidate.get("primary_url") in selected_urls:
   494	            continue
   495	        if candidate.get("source_name", "") in always_read_names:
   496	            continue
   497	        # Journal issue emails get their own digest section (build_journal_watch)
   498	        # — a raw TOC link in "Also considered" would be noise.
   499	        if candidate.get("source_name", "") in journal_names:
   500	            continue
   501	        # Skip candidates whose links were dropped as dead-end redirectors
   502	        # (e.g. McKinsey/Atlantic tokens that resolve to publisher homepage
   503	        # or ad-tracker). A "# → nowhere" link in the digest is worse than
   504	        # omitting the candidate.
   505	        if not candidate.get("primary_url"):
   506	            continue
   507	        scored.append({
   508	            **candidate,
   509	            "triage_score": score_candidate_for_triage(candidate),
   510	        })
   511	
   512	    scored.sort(
   513	        key=lambda candidate: (
   514	            -candidate.get("triage_score", 0),
   515	            candidate.get("source_name", ""),
   516	            candidate.get("headline", ""),
   517	        )
   518	    )
   519	
   520	    # Ensure at least one candidate from each major interest bucket
   521	    queue = []
   522	    buckets_covered = set()
   523	    # First pass: pick the top candidate per bucket
   524	    for candidate in scored:
   525	        bucket = _category_to_bucket(candidate.get("category", "unknown"))
   526	        if bucket not in buckets_covered:
   527	            queue.append(candidate)
   528	            buckets_covered.add(bucket)
   529	    # Second pass: fill remaining slots by score
   530	    for candidate in scored:
   531	        if len(queue) >= limit:
   532	            break
   533	        if candidate not in queue:
   534	            queue.append(candidate)
   535	
   536	    return queue[:limit]
   537

   539	def build_always_read(
   540	    structured_gmail: list[dict],
   541	    selected_articles: list[dict],
   542	) -> list[dict]:
   543	    """Extract candidates from always-read paid sources."""
   544	    always_read_names = get_always_read_names()
   545	    if not always_read_names:
   546	        return []
   547	    selected_urls = {a.get("url") for a in selected_articles}
   548	    results = []
   549	    for candidate in structured_gmail:
   550	        source_name = candidate.get("source_name", "")
   551	        if source_name not in always_read_names:
   552	            continue
   553	        if candidate.get("primary_url") in selected_urls:
   554	            continue
   555	        # Drop URL-less candidates (dead-end redirector) — see build_triage_queue.
   556	        if not candidate.get("primary_url"):
   557	            continue
   558	        results.append(candidate)
   559	    return results

   562	def _journal_picks_from_toc(issue: dict, toc_text: str) -> list[dict]:
   563	    """Ask Claude to nominate 0-2 must-read articles from a journal issue TOC.
   564	
   565	    Returns [] on any failure (missing key, refusal, bad JSON) — the caller
   566	    degrades to listing the issue link, never dropping the section silently.
   567	    """
   568	    today = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
   569	    try:
   570	        client = anthropic.Anthropic()
   571	        resp = client.messages.create(
   572	            model="claude-sonnet-4-6",
   573	            max_tokens=800,
   574	            messages=[{
   575	                "role": "user",
   576	                "content": (
   577	                    f"Today is {today}. Below is the table of contents of a medical/"
   578	                    f"health-policy journal issue ({issue.get('source_name', '')} — "
   579	                    f"{issue.get('headline', '')}).\n\n"
   580	                    "The reader is a healthcare-focused public-equity investor: he cares "
   581	                    "about clinical results that move drug/device markets (obesity/GLP-1, "
   582	                    "oncology, cardiology, gene therapy), healthcare policy & payment "
   583	                    "(CMS, Medicare Advantage, drug pricing), health-system economics, "
   584	                    "and durable scientific insight. He does NOT need clinical-practice "
   585	                    "minutiae, case images, or narrow specialty technique papers.\n\n"
   586	                    "Pick the 0-2 articles from this issue MOST worth his time. Fewer is "
   587	                    "better — pick zero if nothing clears the bar. Reply with ONLY a JSON "
   588	                    "array (no prose): "
   589	                    '[{"title": "...", "url": "...", "why": "one sentence"}] '
   590	                    "(url empty string if not visible in the TOC text).\n\n"
   591	                    f"TOC:\n{toc_text[:6000]}"
   592	                ),
   593	            }],
   594	        )
   595	        picks = _extract_json_array(resp.content)
   596	        clean = []
   597	        for p in picks[:2]:
   598	            if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("title"):
   599	                clean.append({
   600	                    "title": str(p.get("title"))[:200],
   601	                    "url": str(p.get("url") or "")[:500],
   602	                    "why": str(p.get("why") or "")[:300],
   603	                })
   604	        return clean
   605	    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — non-gating by design
   606	        print(f"  [journals] pick failed for {issue.get('headline', '')!r}: {exc}")
   607	        return []
   608	
   609	
   610	def _resolve_pick_urls(picks: list[dict], link_titles: list[dict]) -> None:
   611	    """Fill each pick's url from the issue email's anchor-text->href pairs.
   612	
   613	    The TOC text Claude reads is tag-stripped (inlining ~400-char tracking URLs
   614	    would blow the prompt budget), so picked titles come back URL-less (codex
   615	    2026-07-08). Match by containment either way after lowercasing; leave the
   616	    url empty when nothing matches (the Slack render already handles that)."""
   617	    if not link_titles:
   618	        return
   619	    pairs = [(str(lt.get("text", "")).lower(), str(lt.get("url", "")))
   620	             for lt in link_titles if isinstance(lt, dict) and lt.get("url")]
   621	    for pick in picks:
   622	        if pick.get("url"):
   623	            continue
   624	        title = str(pick.get("title", "")).lower().strip()
   625	        if len(title) < 10:
   626	            continue
   627	        for text, url in pairs:
   628	            if title in text or text in title:
   629	                pick["url"] = url
   630	                break
   631	
   632	
   633	def build_journal_watch(structured_gmail: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
   634	    """Journals section (JP 2026-07-06): each journal-category email is an
   635	    ISSUE (NEJM TOC, Weekend Briefing; Health Affairs once subscribed).
   636	    For each issue, extract the TOC page and have Claude flag the 1-2
   637	    articles worth reading. Failures degrade to a bare issue link.
   638	    """
   639	    journal_names = get_journal_source_names()
   640	    if not journal_names:
   641	        return []
   642	    issues = [c for c in structured_gmail if c.get("source_name") in journal_names]
   643	    if not issues:
   644	        return []
   645	    section("JOURNAL WATCH")
   646	    out = []
   647	    for issue in issues:
   648	        toc_url = issue.get("primary_url") or ""
   649	        picks: list[dict] = []
   650	        # The email body IS the TOC (nejm.org blocks scraping, so the web
   651	        # fetch is only a fallback for e.g. re-processed artifacts that
   652	        # predate body_excerpt).
   653	        toc_text = issue.get("body_excerpt") or ""
   654	        tier = "email_body"
   655	        if not toc_text and toc_url:
   656	            toc_text, tier = fetch_article_text(toc_url, timeout=20)
   657	        if toc_text:
   658	            picks = _journal_picks_from_toc(issue, toc_text)
   659	            _resolve_pick_urls(picks, issue.get("link_titles") or [])
   660	            print(f"  {issue.get('source_name')}: {len(picks)} pick(s) "
   661	                  f"(TOC via {tier})")
   662	        else:
   663	            print(f"  {issue.get('source_name')}: no TOC text — "
   664	                  f"listing issue link only")
   665	        out.append({
   666	            "source_name": issue.get("source_name", ""),
   667	            "headline": issue.get("headline", "(untitled issue)"),
   668	            "url": toc_url,
   669	            "picks": picks,
   670	        })
   671	    return out
   672	
   673	
   674	def _dropped_detail(surfaces) -> list[dict]:
   675	    """Name every item a liveness probe is about to drop.
   676	
   677	    `surfaces` is `[(items, dropped_indices, surface_label), ...]`, read BEFORE
   678	    the lists are filtered — afterwards the indices address different items.
   679	
   680	    The three drop buckets (`always_read`, `triage`, `substack`) recorded only
   681	    a COUNT. Measured on the stored log, `always_read_dropped: 1` appears on 10
   682	    of the 37 runs since 2026-07-01 and every one is anonymous, so the "6 broken
   683	    always-read URLs" on board row #261 could be counted and never fixed. An
   684	    out-of-range index is skipped rather than raised on: this runs inside
   685	    delivery, and a logging detail must never be able to take the digest down.
   686	    """
   687	    out = []
   688	    for items, dropped, surface in surfaces:
   689	        for idx in sorted(dropped or ()):
   690	            if not (0 <= idx < len(items)):
   691	                continue
   692	            item = items[idx] or {}
   693	            out.append({
   694	                "surface": surface,
   695	                "source": item.get("source") or item.get("source_name") or "",
   696	                "headline": (item.get("headline") or item.get("title") or "")[:120],
   697	                "url": item.get("url") or "",
   698	            })
   699	    return out
   700	
   701	
   702	def validate_delivery_urls(
   703	    articles: list[dict],
   704	    triage_queue: list[dict],
   705	    always_read: list[dict],
   706	    substack_items: list[dict],
   702	def validate_delivery_urls(
   703	    articles: list[dict],
   704	    triage_queue: list[dict],
   705	    always_read: list[dict],
   706	    substack_items: list[dict],
   707	    exclude_ids: set[str] | None = None,
   708	) -> tuple[list[dict], list[dict], list[dict], list[dict]]:
   709	    """Probe every URL that is about to ship. Drops broken URLs from
   710	    triage/always_read/substack lists. For main-slot articles (which are
   711	    load-bearing — dropping one would leave an empty slot), logs a loud
   712	    warning but keeps the article so the digest still goes out.
   713	
   714	    Uses `url_resolver.check_urls_live` which treats 404/410 and
   715	    connection errors as broken but keeps 401/403/timeouts (paywalled or
   716	    bot-walled sites that work fine in the user's browser).
   717	    """
   718	    section("DELIVERY URL VALIDATION")
   719	
   720	    # Gather every URL we're about to ship, with its source surface.
   721	    # (surface, index, url)
   722	    urls_to_check: list[tuple[str, int, str]] = []
   723	    for i, a in enumerate(articles):
   724	        url = (a.get("url") or "").strip()
   725	        if url:
   726	            urls_to_check.append(("article", i, url))
   727	    for i, c in enumerate(triage_queue):
   728	        url = (c.get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   729	        if url:
   730	            urls_to_check.append(("triage", i, url))
   731	    for i, c in enumerate(always_read):
   732	        url = (c.get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   733	        if url:
   734	            urls_to_check.append(("always_read", i, url))
   735	    for i, item in enumerate(substack_items):
   736	        url = (item.get("url") or "").strip()
   737	        if url:
   738	            urls_to_check.append(("substack", i, url))
   739	
   740	    unique_urls = list({url for _, _, url in urls_to_check})
   741	    if not unique_urls:
   742	        print("No URLs to validate.")
   743	        return articles, triage_queue, always_read, substack_items
   744	
   745	    print(f"Probing {len(unique_urls)} unique URLs across {len(urls_to_check)} slots...")
   746	    liveness = check_urls_live(unique_urls, timeout=3)
   747	
   748	    broken_articles: list[int] = []
   749	    broken_triage: set[int] = set()
   750	    broken_always_read: set[int] = set()
   751	    broken_substack: set[int] = set()
   752	    for surface, idx, url in urls_to_check:
   753	        if liveness.get(url, True):
   754	            continue
   755	        if surface == "article":
   756	            broken_articles.append(idx)
   757	        elif surface == "triage":
   758	            broken_triage.add(idx)
   759	        elif surface == "always_read":
   760	            broken_always_read.add(idx)
   761	        elif surface == "substack":
   762	            broken_substack.add(idx)
   763	
   764	    # A broken main-slot URL used to ship as-is with a warning, because dropping
   765	    # it would leave an empty slot. But a dead link in the top slot is a
   766	    # reader-visible failure on a run that still reports `ok` (JP, 2026-07-19).
   767	    # So: promote the best still-live "also considered" candidate into the slot
   768	    # instead. Shipping broken is now only the last resort when no eligible
   769	    # substitute exists — never a silent degradation either way.
   770	    promoted_triage: set[int] = set()
   771	    substituted: list[dict] = []
   772	    still_broken: list[int] = []
   773	
   774	    # Sources already spoken for, so a substitute can't duplicate one (the same
   775	    # constraint validate_selected_articles enforces at selection time).
   776	    taken_sources = {
   777	        str(a.get("source", "")).strip().casefold()
   778	        for i, a in enumerate(articles)
   779	        if i not in broken_articles and str(a.get("source", "")).strip()
   780	    }
   781	    taken_urls = {
   782	        (a.get("url") or "").strip()
   783	        for i, a in enumerate(articles) if i not in broken_articles
   784	    }
   785	
   786	    # Best-first pool: live-URL triage candidates, highest triage_score first.
   787	    # build_triage_queue reorders for bucket coverage, so re-sort by score here.
   788	    #
   789	    # `exclude_ids` is load-bearing (codex 2026-07-20): select_articles() filters
   790	    # recently-delivered candidates, but build_triage_queue() does NOT — it is
   791	    # rebuilt from the full candidate set. Without this check a candidate that
   792	    # the cross-run dedupe deliberately kept out of today's selection could be
   793	    # promoted straight into a main slot and re-delivered.
   794	    _excluded = exclude_ids or set()
   795	    pool = sorted(
   796	        (
   797	            i for i in range(len(triage_queue))
   798	            if i not in broken_triage
   799	            and (triage_queue[i].get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   800	            and triage_queue[i].get("candidate_id") not in _excluded
   801	        ),
   802	        key=lambda i: -int(triage_queue[i].get("triage_score") or 0),
   803	    )
   804	
   805	    for idx in sorted(broken_articles, key=lambda i: articles[i].get("slot", 0)):
   806	        a = articles[idx]
   807	        slot = a.get("slot", "?")
   808	        sub_i = None
   809	        for i in pool:
   810	            if i in promoted_triage:
   811	                continue
   812	            c = triage_queue[i]
   813	            src = str(c.get("source_name", "")).strip()
   814	            url = (c.get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   815	            if not src or not url:
   816	                continue
   817	            if src.casefold() in taken_sources or url in taken_urls:
   818	                continue
   819	            sub_i = i
   820	            break
   821	
   822	        if sub_i is None:
   823	            still_broken.append(idx)
   824	            print(
   825	                f"  WARNING: main slot {slot} URL is broken and NO eligible "
   826	                f"substitute was available — shipping broken to preserve the "
   827	                f"slot. Headline: {a.get('headline', '')[:60]}"
   828	            )
   829	            print(f"    URL: {a.get('url', '')}")
   830	            continue
   831	
   832	        c = triage_queue[sub_i]
   833	        src = str(c.get("source_name", "")).strip()
   834	        url = (c.get("primary_url") or "").strip()
   835	        # Triage candidates carry no editorial summary/why-it-matters (those are
   836	        # written by the selection LLM). Rather than spend a second LLM call on
   837	        # a fallback path, use the candidate's own snippet and say plainly in
   838	        # the digest that this is a substitution — honest beats fabricated.
   839	        snippet = str(c.get("snippet") or c.get("body_excerpt") or "").strip()
   840	        snippet = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", snippet)[:400]
   841	        articles[idx] = {
   842	            "article_id": article_id_for(url, src),
   843	            "headline": str(c.get("headline") or "(untitled)").strip(),
   844	            "source": src,
   845	            "url": url,
   846	            "slot": a.get("slot"),
   847	            "summary": snippet or "(no summary available — promoted from the "
   848	                                  "also-considered queue)",
   849	            "why_it_matters": (
   850	                f"Promoted from 'also considered' because the original slot-{slot} "
   851	                f"pick ({a.get('source', 'unknown source')}) shipped a dead link."
   852	            ),
   853	            "signal_tags": [],
   854	            "reading_time": "N/A",
   855	        }
   856	        promoted_triage.add(sub_i)
   857	        taken_sources.add(src.casefold())
   858	        taken_urls.add(url)
   859	        substituted.append({
   860	            "slot": a.get("slot"),
   861	            "broken_source": a.get("source", ""),
   862	            "broken_url": a.get("url", ""),
   863	            "substitute_source": src,
   864	            "substitute_headline": str(c.get("headline") or "")[:120],
   865	            "substitute_url": url,
   866	        })
   867	        print(
   868	            f"  SUBSTITUTED main slot {slot}: broken link from "
   869	            f"{a.get('source', '?')} replaced with {src} — "
   870	            f"{str(c.get('headline') or '')[:60]}"
   871	        )
   872	
   873	    def _filter(
   874	        items: list[dict],
   875	        dropped: set[int],
   876	        label: str,
   877	        silent: set[int] | None = None,
   878	    ) -> list[dict]:
   879	        """Remove `dropped` indices. Indices in `silent` are removed without a
   880	        "URL failed liveness probe" line — used for promoted candidates, which
   881	        leave the queue for a different (non-failure) reason."""
   882	        if not dropped:
   883	            return items
   884	        skip_log = silent or set()
   885	        for idx in sorted(dropped):
   886	            if idx in skip_log:
   887	                continue
   888	            c = items[idx]
   889	            headline = c.get("headline") or c.get("subject", "Untitled")
   890	            print(f"  Dropping {label}: {headline[:60]} — URL failed liveness probe")
   891	        return [c for i, c in enumerate(items) if i not in dropped]
   892	
   893	    # Promoted candidates leave the triage queue so they don't appear twice in
   894	    # the digest (once in a main slot, once under "also considered").
   895	    for i in sorted(promoted_triage):
   896	        print(
   897	            f"  Removing from triage (promoted to a main slot): "
   898	            f"{str(triage_queue[i].get('headline') or '')[:60]}"
   899	        )
   900	    # Capture WHICH items are about to be dropped, before `_filter` reindexes
   901	    # the lists — afterwards the indices no longer address the same items.
   902	    dropped_detail = _dropped_detail([
   903	        (always_read, broken_always_read, "always_read"),
   904	        (triage_queue, broken_triage - promoted_triage, "triage"),
   905	        (substack_items, broken_substack, "substack"),
   906	    ])
   907	
   908	    triage_queue = _filter(
   909	        triage_queue, broken_triage | promoted_triage, "triage", silent=promoted_triage
   910	    )
   911	    always_read = _filter(always_read, broken_always_read, "always-read")
   912	    substack_items = _filter(substack_items, broken_substack, "substack")
   913	
   914	    total_broken = (
   915	        len(broken_articles) + len(broken_triage)
   916	        + len(broken_always_read) + len(broken_substack)
   917	    )
   918	    if total_broken == 0:
   919	        print("All URLs passed liveness check.")
   920	    else:
   921	        print(
   922	            f"Summary: {total_broken} broken URL(s) detected "
   923	            f"({len(substituted)} main slots substituted, "
   924	            f"{len(still_broken)} main shipped broken, "
   925	            f"{len(broken_triage)} triage dropped, "
   926	            f"{len(broken_always_read)} always-read dropped, "
   927	            f"{len(broken_substack)} substack dropped)."
   928	        )
   929	
   930	    # Append this run's validation counts to the log so the Friday weekly
   931	    # report can surface URL-health trends.
   932	    today = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
   933	    log_path = "artifacts/url_validation_log.json"
   934	    log = load_json(log_path, [])
   935	    log.append({
   936	        "date": today,
   937	        "checked_unique": len(unique_urls),
   938	        "checked_slots": len(urls_to_check),
   939	        "broken": {
   940	            # `article_warnings` now means "broken AND no substitute was
   941	            # available, so it shipped broken" — the genuinely bad case.
   942	            # Slots we rescued by promotion are counted separately.
   943	            "article_warnings": len(still_broken),
   944	            "article_substituted": len(substituted),
   945	            "triage_dropped": len(broken_triage),
   946	            "always_read_dropped": len(broken_always_read),
   947	            "substack_dropped": len(broken_substack),
   948	        },
   949	        # Detail so the weekly report can name which sources ship broken URLs.
   950	        # `warned_articles` still reflects post-substitution state: these are
   951	        # the ones the reader actually received with a dead link.
   952	        "warned_articles": [
   953	            {
   954	                "source": articles[i].get("source", ""),
   955	                "headline": (articles[i].get("headline") or "")[:120],
   956	                "url": articles[i].get("url", ""),
   957	            }
   958	            for i in still_broken
   959	        ],
   960	        "substituted_articles": substituted,
   961	        # The three DROP buckets carried a count and nothing else, so a
   962	        # dropped item was unidentifiable after the fact. Measured on the
   963	        # stored log: `always_read_dropped: 1` on 10 of the 37 runs since
   964	        # 2026-07-01, every one of them anonymous — which is why the "6 broken
   965	        # always-read URLs" on board row #261 could be counted but never fixed.
   966	        # A count you cannot act on is not an observation.
   967	        "dropped_items": dropped_detail,
   968	    })
   969	    save_json(log_path, log)
   970	    print(f"Logged validation stats to {log_path}")
   971	
   972	    return articles, triage_queue, always_read, substack_items
   973

   975	def validate_selected_articles(articles: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
  1204	def feedback_check() -> dict:
  1205	    section("FEEDBACK CHECK")
  1206	    feedback = load_json("feedback_log.json", [])
  1207	    result = {"low_scores": [], "should_rewrite": False}
  1208	
  1209	    if not feedback:
  1210	        print("No feedback yet.")
  1211	        return result
  1212	
  1213	    # Check yesterday's ratings
  1214	    today = datetime.now(timezone.utc).date()
  1215	    yesterday = today - timedelta(days=1)
  1216	    yesterday_entries = [
  1217	        f for f in feedback
  1218	        if f.get("date", "")[:10] == str(yesterday)
  1219	    ]
  1220	    low = [f for f in yesterday_entries if f.get("score", 2) == 1]
  1221	    if low:
  1222	        result["low_scores"] = low
  1223	        print(f"Found {len(low)} low-rated articles from yesterday")
  1224	
  1225	    # Check if 7+ days of feedback → trigger rewrite
  1226	    unique_dates = set(f.get("date", "")[:10] for f in feedback)
  1227	    if len(unique_dates) >= 7:
  1228	        result["should_rewrite"] = True
  1229	        print("7+ days of feedback accumulated — will trigger criteria rewrite")
  1230	    else:
  1231	        print(f"{len(unique_dates)} day(s) of feedback so far (need 7 for rewrite)")
  1232	
  1233	    return result

  1236	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1237	# [ARTICLE SELECTION]
  1238	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1239	
  1240	def _extract_json_array(content_blocks) -> list:
  1241	    """Pull the article-shortlist JSON array out of a tool-use response.
  1242	
  1243	    Robust to the failure modes that made the old single greedy `\\[.*\\]` regex
  1244	    brittle: with the web_search tool, `content` interleaves narration text
  1245	    blocks (which can contain stray `[...]` like "[1]") before the final answer.
  1246	    Strategy: scan TEXT blocks in REVERSE (the JSON answer comes last), strip
  1247	    ```json code fences, then try each balanced top-level `[...]` span (last
  1248	    first) until one parses as a list. Returns [] if nothing parses (e.g. the
  1249	    array was truncated at max_tokens)."""
  1250	    texts = [b.text for b in content_blocks if getattr(b, "type", None) == "text"]
  1251	    for text in reversed(texts):
  1252	        t = text.strip()
  1253	        if "```" in t:
  1254	            # keep only fenced bodies if present (handles ```json … ```)
  1255	            fences = re.findall(r"```(?:json)?\s*(.*?)```", t, re.DOTALL)
  1256	            if fences:
  1257	                t = "\n".join(fences)
  1258	        opens = [i for i, c in enumerate(t) if c == "["]
  1259	        for s in reversed(opens):
  1260	            depth = 0
  1261	            for e in range(s, len(t)):
  1262	                if t[e] == "[":
  1263	                    depth += 1
  1264	                elif t[e] == "]":
  1265	                    depth -= 1
  1266	                    if depth == 0:
  1267	                        try:
  1268	                            parsed = json.loads(t[s:e + 1])
  1269	                            # Require the article-object shape: a non-empty list
  1270	                            # of dicts. Without the all-dicts check the reverse
  1271	                            # scan can return a *nested* string array (e.g. a
  1272	                            # "signal_tags": ["ai","biotech"] span, hit first
  1273	                            # because it's the last "[") as if it were the
  1274	                            # shortlist — then `a.get()` downstream blows up with
  1275	                            # AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'.
  1276	                            if (isinstance(parsed, list) and parsed
  1277	                                    and all(isinstance(x, dict) for x in parsed)):
  1278	                                return parsed
  1279	                        except json.JSONDecodeError:
  1280	                            pass
  1281	                        break  # this open didn't yield valid JSON; try an earlier one
  1282	    return []
  1283	
  1284	
  1285	def select_articles(

  1437	def _fetch_with_trafilatura(url: str, timeout: int = 15) -> str | None:
  1438	    """Try to extract article text using trafilatura (local, free)."""
  1439	    import trafilatura

  1441	    try:
  1442	        downloaded = trafilatura.fetch_url(url)
  1443	        if not downloaded:
  1444	            print(f"    Trafilatura: fetch returned nothing")
  1445	            return None
  1446	        text = trafilatura.extract(downloaded, include_comments=False, include_tables=False)
  1447	        if text and len(text) > 500:
  1448	            print(f"    Trafilatura: extracted {len(text)} chars")
  1449	            return text
  1450	        print(f"    Trafilatura: too little text ({len(text) if text else 0} chars)")
  1451	    except Exception as e:
  1452	        print(f"    Trafilatura failed: {e}")
  1453	    return None

  1456	def _fetch_with_jina(url: str) -> str | None:
  1457	    """Fall back to Jina Reader API (free, handles JS rendering)."""
  1458	    print(f"    Falling back to Jina Reader...")

  1460	        resp = requests.get(
  1461	            f"https://r.jina.ai/{url}",
  1462	            headers={"Accept": "text/plain"},
  1463	            timeout=30,
  1464	        )
  1465	        resp.raise_for_status()
  1466	        text = resp.text.strip()

  1468	            print(f"    Jina Reader: extracted {len(text)} chars")
  1469	            return text
  1470	        print(f"    Jina Reader: too little text ({len(text)} chars)")
  1471	    except Exception as e:
  1472	        print(f"    Jina Reader failed: {e}")
  1473	    return None
  1474

  1476	def _fetch_with_tavily(url: str) -> str | None:
  1477	    """Last resort: Tavily extract API for the hardest pages."""

  1489	        )
  1490	        resp.raise_for_status()
  1491	        data = resp.json()
  1492	        results = data.get("results", [])

  1494	            text = results[0]["raw_content"]

  1502	_PAYWALL_PATTERNS = re.compile(
  1503	    r"(are you a robot|captcha|subscribe to continue|sign in to read|"
  1504	    r"create a free account|this content is for subscribers|"
  1505	    r"please log in|paywall|access denied|unusual activity)",
  1506	    re.IGNORECASE,
  1507	)
  1508	
  1509	# Strong paywall markers that reliably indicate the BODY is gated even when the
  1510	# extractor grabbed a long-enough intro/teaser to clear the generic length gate —
  1511	# the STAT+ case (JP's top HC source was being extracted as a teaser + CTA,
  1512	# passing to the verifier, getting rejected as "no substance", and dropped
  1513	# instead of falling back to the newsletter snippet). 2026-07-11.
  1514	_HARD_PAYWALL_PATTERNS = re.compile(
  1515	    r"(STAT\+|subscribers?[- ]only|already a subscriber|subscribe to read|"
  1516	    r"unlock this (article|story)|to continue reading,|"
  1517	    r"this (article|story|content) is (exclusive|for subscribers))",
  1518	    re.IGNORECASE,
  1519	)

  1522	def _is_paywall_stub(text: str) -> bool:
  1523	    """Detect paywall, captcha, or bot-wall pages masquerading as article text."""
  1524	    # Generic wall + short body.
  1525	    if len(text) < 1500 and _PAYWALL_PATTERNS.search(text):
  1526	        return True
  1527	    # Strong paywall markers (STAT+, "subscribers only") flag even a longer intro
  1528	    # teaser. Length ceiling so a genuinely-full article that merely *mentions* a
  1529	    # paywall isn't nuked — and a false positive here only routes to snippet-only
  1530	    # verification (lighter bar), never a hard drop, so the bias is toward recovery.
  1531	    if len(text) < 4000 and _HARD_PAYWALL_PATTERNS.search(text):
  1532	        return True
  1533	    return False

  1536	def fetch_article_text(url: str, timeout: int = 15) -> tuple[str | None, str]:
  1537	    """Fetch article text with 3-tier fallback: trafilatura -> Jina -> Tavily.
  1538	
  1539	    Returns (text, extraction_tier) where tier is one of:
  1540	    'trafilatura', 'jina', 'tavily', or 'none'.
  1541	    """
  1542	    text = _fetch_with_trafilatura(url, timeout)
  1543	    if text and not _is_paywall_stub(text):
  1544	        if len(text) > 6000:
  1545	            text = text[:6000] + "\n[...truncated]"
  1546	        return text, "trafilatura"
  1547	
  1548	    text = _fetch_with_jina(url)
  1549	    if text and not _is_paywall_stub(text):
  1550	        if len(text) > 6000:
  1551	            text = text[:6000] + "\n[...truncated]"
  1552	        return text, "jina"
  1553	
  1554	    text = _fetch_with_tavily(url)
  1555	    if text and not _is_paywall_stub(text):
  1556	        if len(text) > 6000:
  1557	            text = text[:6000] + "\n[...truncated]"
  1558	        return text, "tavily"
  1559	
  1560	    if text and _is_paywall_stub(text):
  1561	        print(f"    Detected paywall/bot-wall stub — discarding")
  1562	
  1563	    return None, "none"
