   182	def fetch_newsletters(hours_back: int = 26) -> list[dict]:
   183	    """Fetch newsletter emails from the last N hours.
   184	
   185	    Returns list of dicts with keys:
   186	        sender, sender_email, subject, snippet, date, urls,
   187	        source_name, tier, category, priority
   188	    """
   189	    service = get_gmail_service()
   190	    cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=hours_back)
   191	    date_query = cutoff.strftime("%Y/%m/%d")
   192	
   193	    # Build OR query for all tracked senders
   194	    sender_emails = get_all_sender_emails()
   195	    from_clauses = " OR ".join(f"from:{e}" for e in sender_emails)
   196	    query = f"after:{date_query} ({from_clauses})"
   197	
   198	    results = []
   199	    page_token = None
   200	
   201	    while True:
   202	        resp = service.users().messages().list(
   203	            userId="me", q=query, maxResults=100, pageToken=page_token
   204	        ).execute()
   205	
   206	        messages = resp.get("messages", [])
   207	        for msg_stub in messages:
   208	            msg = service.users().messages().get(
   209	                userId="me", id=msg_stub["id"], format="full"
   210	            ).execute()
   211	
   212	            headers = {h["name"].lower(): h["value"] for h in msg["payload"]["headers"]}
   213	            sender_raw = headers.get("from", "")
   214	            _, sender_email = parseaddr(sender_raw)
   215	            sender_email = sender_email.lower()
   216	
   217	            subject = headers.get("subject", "(no subject)")
   218	            date_str = headers.get("date", "")
   219	            snippet = msg.get("snippet", "")
   220	
   221	            # Match against sources
   222	            source = get_source(sender_email)
   223	
   224	            # Per-source subject whitelist: if a source specifies `subject_allow`,
   225	            # drop any email whose subject doesn't match one of the patterns.
   226	            # Used to filter marketing/promo emails from paid newsletters whose
   227	            # real-content subjects follow a known shape (e.g. VII).
   228	            if source and source.get("subject_allow"):
   229	                patterns = source["subject_allow"]
   230	                if not any(re.search(p, subject) for p in patterns):
   231	                    continue
   232	
   233	            # Extract HTML body for URL parsing
   234	            html_body = _extract_body(msg["payload"])
   235	            urls = extract_urls_from_html(html_body) if html_body else []
   236	            # Resolve newsletter click-tracking redirector URLs (BioSpace,
   237	            # SendGrid, etc.) to their canonical destinations. No-op for
   238	            # URLs that aren't on known redirector hosts.
   239	            if urls:
   240	                from url_resolver import resolve_urls
   241	                urls = resolve_urls(urls)
   242	            # Journal issue emails ARE the table of contents — nejm.org blocks
   243	            # scraping (all three extraction tiers fail), so the must-read
   244	            # picker reads the email body itself. link_titles keeps the anchor
   245	            # text -> href pairs so picked titles can be resolved back to real
   246	            # URLs (the text excerpt strips tags, and inlining ~400-char
   247	            # tracking URLs into the LLM prompt would blow its budget).
   248	            # Both kept journal-only to avoid bloating every candidate artifact.
   249	            body_excerpt = ""
   250	            link_titles: list[dict] = []
   251	            if source and source.get("category") == "journals" and html_body:
   252	                body_excerpt = _html_to_text_excerpt(html_body, limit=8000)
   253	                link_titles = _extract_link_titles(html_body, cap=40)
   254	
   255	            results.append({
   256	                "sender": sender_raw,
   257	                "sender_email": sender_email,
   258	                "subject": subject,
   259	                "snippet": snippet,
   260	                "date": date_str,
   261	                "urls": urls[:20],  # cap to avoid noise
   262	                "source_name": source["name"] if source else "Unknown",
   263	                "tier": source["tier"] if source else 0,
   264	                "category": source["category"] if source else "unknown",
   265	                "priority": source["priority"] if source else "normal",
   266	                "body_excerpt": body_excerpt,
   267	                "link_titles": link_titles,
   268	            })
   269	
   270	        page_token = resp.get("nextPageToken")
   271	        if not page_token:
   272	            break
   273	
   274	    return results

   276	
   277	def _pick_substack_post_url(urls: list[str]) -> str | None:
   278	    """Pick the canonical post URL from a Substack email's link list.
   279	
   280	    Substack emails contain many wrapper/app-link/reaction URLs. The real post
   281	    URL typically lives on the publication's domain and has a `/p/<slug>` path
   282	    (or a custom-domain equivalent). Fall back to the first http(s) link that
   283	    isn't an app-link/share URL.
   284	    """
   285	    fallback = None
   286	    for url in urls:
   287	        try:
   288	            parsed = urlsplit(url)
   289	        except ValueError:
   290	            continue
   291	        if parsed.scheme not in {"http", "https"}:
   292	            continue
   293	        host = parsed.netloc.lower()
   294	        path = parsed.path
   295	        if "app-link" in path or path.startswith("/api/") or "/action/" in path:
   296	            continue
   297	        if host.endswith("substack.com") and "/p/" in path:
   298	            return url
   299	        if host.endswith("substack.com") and path.startswith("/p/"):
   300	            return url
   301	        if not host.endswith("substack.com") and "/p/" in path:
   302	            # custom-domain Substack post
   303	            return url
   304	        if fallback is None:
   305	            fallback = url
   306	    return fallback
   307	
   308	
   309	def fetch_substack_emails(hours_back: int = 26) -> list[dict]:
   310	    """Fetch all @substack.com emails in the window, regardless of sources.py.
   311	
   312	    Used to render the daily digest's Substack section so the user can spot
   313	    newsletters worth promoting to always_read.
   314	
   315	    Returns list of dicts with keys: sender_name, sender_email, subject, url, date.
   316	    """
   317	    service = get_gmail_service()
   318	    cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=hours_back)
   319	    date_query = cutoff.strftime("%Y/%m/%d")
   320	    query = f"after:{date_query} from:substack.com"
   321	
   322	    results: list[dict] = []
   323	    page_token = None
   324	    while True:
   325	        resp = service.users().messages().list(
   326	            userId="me", q=query, maxResults=100, pageToken=page_token
   327	        ).execute()
   328	
   329	        for msg_stub in resp.get("messages", []):
   330	            msg = service.users().messages().get(
   331	                userId="me", id=msg_stub["id"], format="full"
   332	            ).execute()
   333	            headers = {h["name"].lower(): h["value"] for h in msg["payload"]["headers"]}
   334	            sender_raw = headers.get("from", "")
   335	            sender_name, sender_email = parseaddr(sender_raw)
   336	            sender_email = sender_email.lower()
   337	            if not sender_email.endswith("@substack.com"):
   338	                continue
   339	
   340	            subject = headers.get("subject", "(no subject)")
   341	            date_str = headers.get("date", "")
   342	
   343	            html_body = _extract_body(msg["payload"])
   344	            urls = extract_urls_from_html(html_body) if html_body else []
   345	            post_url = _pick_substack_post_url(urls) if urls else None
   346	
   347	            results.append({
   348	                "sender_name": sender_name or sender_email,
   349	                "sender_email": sender_email,
   350	                "subject": subject,
   351	                "url": post_url or "",
   352	                "date": date_str,
   353	            })
   354	
   355	        page_token = resp.get("nextPageToken")
   356	        if not page_token:
   357	            break
   358	
   359	    return results

   361	
   362	def _extract_link_titles(html: str, cap: int = 40) -> list[dict]:
   363	    """Anchor text -> href pairs for journal TOC emails. Only anchors whose
   364	    visible text looks like an article title (>= 15 chars) are kept, so
   365	    'View in browser' / 'SUBSCRIBE' chrome is skipped."""
   366	    soup = BeautifulSoup(html, "html.parser")
   367	    out: list[dict] = []
   368	    seen: set[str] = set()
   369	    for a in soup.find_all("a", href=True):
   370	        text = " ".join(a.get_text(" ", strip=True).split())
   371	        href = a["href"].strip()
   372	        if len(text) < 15 or not href.startswith("http"):
   373	            continue
   374	        key = text.lower()
   375	        if key in seen:
   376	            continue
   377	        seen.add(key)
   378	        out.append({"text": text[:220], "url": href[:1000]})
   379	        if len(out) >= cap:
   380	            break
   381	    return out
   382	
   383	
   384	def _html_to_text_excerpt(html: str, limit: int = 8000) -> str:
   385	    """Crude HTML→text for journal TOC emails: strip tags/styles, collapse
   386	    whitespace. Good enough for an LLM to read article titles/sections."""
   387	    text = re.sub(r"(?is)<(script|style)[^>]*>.*?</\1>", " ", html)
   388	    text = re.sub(r"(?i)<br\s*/?>|</p>|</div>|</tr>|</h[1-6]>", "\n", text)
   389	    text = re.sub(r"<[^>]+>", " ", text)
   390	    text = unescape_html(text)
   391	    text = re.sub(r"[ \t]+", " ", text)
   392	    text = re.sub(r"\n\s*\n+", "\n", text)
   393	    return text.strip()[:limit]
   394	
   395	
   396	def _extract_body(payload: dict) -> str | None:
   397	    """Recursively extract HTML body from Gmail payload."""
   398	    if payload.get("mimeType") == "text/html":
   399	        data = payload.get("body", {}).get("data", "")
   400	        if data:
   401	            return base64.urlsafe_b64decode(data).decode("utf-8", errors="replace")
   402	
   403	    for part in payload.get("parts", []):
   404	        result = _extract_body(part)
   405	        if result:
   406	            return result
   407	    return None
     1	"""Resolve newsletter tracking-redirect URLs to their canonical destinations.
     2	
     3	Some newsletter senders (e.g. BioSpace via HubSpot, Endpoints via SendGrid)
     4	wrap every link in a click-tracking redirector. The wrapped URLs are 600+
     5	chars and clutter every downstream surface — Slack, Pages, Gmail, Reader.
     6	
     7	This module follows redirects exactly once per URL, caches the result, and
     8	returns the cleaned canonical URL. On any failure (timeout, non-2xx, network
     9	error) it returns the original URL unchanged so the pipeline never breaks.
    10	"""
    11	
    12	from __future__ import annotations
    13	
    14	import json
    15	import os
    16	import re
    17	from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
    18	from urllib.parse import urlsplit
    19	
    20	import requests
    21	
    22	from gmail_reader import clean_url
    23	
    24	CACHE_PATH = "url_resolution_cache.json"
    25	
    26	# Hosts whose URLs are click-tracking redirectors AND that we attempt to
    27	# resolve with a single HTTP request. Only URLs on these hosts trigger a
    28	# network call — everything else passes through untouched.
    29	#
    30	# NOT included (intentionally):
    31	#   - marketing.biospace.com — HubSpot wraps tracking URLs in a JS
    32	#     bot-detection landing page that blocks server-side resolution.
    33	#     There is no <noscript>, meta-refresh, or canonical URL fallback;
    34	#     defeating it would require a real headless browser. The Slack
    35	#     chunker handles these long URLs downstream as a safety net.
    36	#
    37	# Note: email.mckinsey.com and link.theatlantic.com tokens frequently fail
    38	# to resolve to the real article (token consumed / cookie-gated) and land
    39	# on the publisher homepage or an ad-tracker. We still try to resolve them
    40	# here, but the dead-end check below drops the URL so a broken link never
    41	# ships in the digest.
    42	# Explicit hosts we've confirmed are click-tracking redirectors. Kept for
    43	# readability / debugging; the shape-based pattern below catches most new
    44	# tracker subdomains automatically.
    45	KNOWN_REDIRECTORS = re.compile(
    46	    r"^("
    47	    r"[a-z0-9]+\.ct\.sendgrid\.net"
    48	    r"|email\.mckinsey\.com"
    49	    r"|link\.theatlantic\.com"
    50	    r"|qtx\.omeclk\.com"
    51	    r"|links\.morningbrew\.com"
    52	    r"|links\.message\.bloomberg\.com"
    53	    r"|urldefense\.com"
    54	    r")$",
    55	    re.IGNORECASE,
    56	)
    57	
    58	# Shape-based tracker detection. Any URL whose subdomain looks like a
    59	# click/email tracker gets resolution + dead-end checking, regardless of
    60	# whether we've seen the host before. This is what makes the system
    61	# self-healing for new newsletter platforms.
    62	LIKELY_TRACKER_PATTERN = re.compile(
    63	    r"^(links?|email|mail|trk|click|go|m|r|cl|e)\d*\.[a-z0-9-]+\.[a-z]{2,}$",
    64	    re.IGNORECASE,
    65	)
    66	
    67	# Ad-tech tracker hosts — if a redirector resolves to one of these, the
    68	# URL never leads to a readable article. Dropped.
    69	DEAD_END_HOSTS = re.compile(
    70	    r"^(www\.)?(p\.)?liadm\.com$|\.liadm\.com$",
    71	    re.IGNORECASE,
    72	)
    73	
    74	REQUEST_TIMEOUT = 6
    75	# Use a realistic browser UA — some tracking redirectors bot-sniff and
    76	# return a 200 landing page to the bot UA instead of a 30x to the article.
    77	USER_AGENT = (
    78	    "Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) "
    79	    "AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) "
    80	    "Chrome/122.0.0.0 Safari/537.36"
    81	)
    82	MAX_WORKERS = 8
    83	
    84	
    85	def _load_cache() -> dict[str, str]:
    86	    if not os.path.exists(CACHE_PATH):
    87	        return {}
    88	    try:
    89	        with open(CACHE_PATH, encoding="utf-8") as f:
    90	            data = json.load(f)
    91	        return data if isinstance(data, dict) else {}
    92	    except Exception:
    93	        return {}
    94	
    95	
    96	def _save_cache(cache: dict[str, str]) -> None:
    97	    try:
    98	        with open(CACHE_PATH, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    99	            json.dump(cache, f, indent=2, sort_keys=True)
   100	    except Exception as e:
   101	        print(f"url_resolver: failed to save cache: {e}")
   102	
   103	
   104	def is_redirector(url: str) -> bool:
   105	    try:
   106	        host = urlsplit(url).netloc
   107	    except Exception:
   108	        return False
   109	    return bool(KNOWN_REDIRECTORS.match(host) or LIKELY_TRACKER_PATTERN.match(host))
   110	
   111	
   112	def _is_dead_end(final_url: str) -> bool:
   113	    """Return True if a resolved URL is a dead end. Two rules:
   114	      1. Final host is a known ad-tracker (liadm.com family, etc.).
   115	      2. Final path is empty or `/` — a redirector that lands on the bare
   116	         publisher homepage has lost the article reference by definition.
   117	
   118	    Rule 2 is intentionally generic: it catches McKinsey, Fierce, and any
   119	    future publisher whose tokens get consumed/expire — no per-host config.
   120	    """
   121	    try:
   122	        parsed = urlsplit(final_url)
   123	    except Exception:
   124	        return False
   125	    host = parsed.netloc.lower()
   126	    path = parsed.path or "/"
   127	    if DEAD_END_HOSTS.search(host):
   128	        return True
   129	    if path in ("", "/"):
   130	        return True
   131	    return False
   132	
   133	
   134	# Status codes that mean "URL is definitely broken". 401/403 are treated as
   135	# OK because they're paywalls (Bloomberg, WSJ, FT) — the URL itself is valid
   136	# and the user can access it with their subscription.
   137	HARD_FAILURE_CODES = {404, 410}
   138	
   139	
   140	def check_url_live(url: str, timeout: int = 3) -> bool:
   141	    """Quick liveness probe used right before delivery.
   142	
   143	    Returns False only for confident failures (404/410, DNS/connection
   144	    errors). Timeouts, 5xx, and other ambiguous responses return True so
   145	    bot-walled sites (STAT, BioSpace) aren't accidentally dropped — the
   146	    user can still click through in their browser.
   147	    """
   148	    if not url or not url.startswith(("http://", "https://")):
   149	        return False
   150	    headers = {"User-Agent": USER_AGENT}
   151	    try:
   152	        resp = requests.head(url, allow_redirects=True, timeout=timeout, headers=headers)
   153	        # 405 = explicit "no HEAD". 404/410 from HEAD can also lie: fda.gov
   154	        # serves 200 on GET but 404 on HEAD from GH Actions IP ranges, which
   155	        # was generating ~90% of our main-slot URL warnings. Verify with GET
   156	        # before declaring a HARD_FAILURE code.
   157	        if resp.status_code in (405, *HARD_FAILURE_CODES):
   158	            resp = requests.get(
   159	                url, allow_redirects=True, timeout=timeout, headers=headers, stream=True
   160	            )
   161	            resp.close()
   162	        if resp.status_code in HARD_FAILURE_CODES:
   163	            return False
   164	        # A live 2xx/3xx that lands on the bare publisher homepage (path '/')
   165	        # or a known ad-tracker host has lost the article reference — the same
   166	        # dead-end rule resolve_urls applies at ingest. Without this, an
   167	        # expired tracker that 200s onto the homepage sails through the
   168	        # pre-delivery liveness probe and ships a link to nowhere.
   169	        final_url = resp.url or url
   170	        if _is_dead_end(final_url):
   171	            return False
   172	        return True
   173	    except requests.exceptions.Timeout:
   174	        return True  # unknown — don't drop on bot-walled sites
   175	    except requests.exceptions.ConnectionError:
   176	        return False  # DNS fail / connection refused = broken
   177	    except Exception:
   178	        return True  # unknown error — conservative: keep the URL
   179	
   180	
   181	def check_urls_live(urls: list[str], timeout: int = 3) -> dict[str, bool]:
   182	    """Parallel liveness probe. Returns {url: is_live} for each input URL."""
   183	    if not urls:
   184	        return {}
   185	    results: dict[str, bool] = {}
   186	    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=MAX_WORKERS) as pool:
   187	        futures = {pool.submit(check_url_live, u, timeout): u for u in urls}
   188	        for fut in as_completed(futures):
   189	            url = futures[fut]
   190	            try:
   191	                results[url] = fut.result()
   192	            except Exception:
   193	                results[url] = True
   194	    return results
   195	
   196	
   197	# Sentinel returned by _resolve_one when the fetch failed for a transient
   198	# transport reason (timeout, DNS blip, connection reset). Distinct from a
   199	# genuine resolution so the caller can keep the original URL in the output
   200	# WITHOUT caching it — a transient blip must not become a permanent no-op
   201	# that suppresses every future retry of that redirector.
   202	TRANSIENT_FAILURE = object()
   203	
   204	
   205	def _resolve_one(url: str):
   206	    """Follow redirects on a single URL.
   207	
   208	    Returns the cleaned final URL on a genuine resolution, an empty string if
   209	    the redirector resolves to a dead end, or the ``TRANSIENT_FAILURE``
   210	    sentinel if the fetch failed for a transient transport reason. Callers
   211	    must NOT cache a ``TRANSIENT_FAILURE`` — only real resolutions are cached
   212	    so a network blip doesn't permanently poison the cache with a no-op."""
   213	    headers = {"User-Agent": USER_AGENT}
   214	    try:
   215	        # HEAD is cheaper but some redirectors only honor GET. Try HEAD first.
   216	        resp = requests.head(url, allow_redirects=True, timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT, headers=headers)
   217	        if resp.status_code >= 400 or not resp.url or resp.url == url:
   218	            resp = requests.get(
   219	                url, allow_redirects=True, timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT, headers=headers, stream=True
   220	            )
   221	            resp.close()
   222	        final = resp.url or url
   223	        if not final.startswith(("http://", "https://")):
   224	            return url
   225	        if _is_dead_end(final):
   226	            return ""
   227	        return clean_url(final)
   228	    except Exception:
   229	        return TRANSIENT_FAILURE
   230	
   231	
   232	def resolve_urls(urls: list[str]) -> list[str]:
   233	    """Resolve any redirector URLs in the list. Order is preserved.
   234	
   235	    URLs not on known redirector hosts pass through untouched. Resolved URLs
   236	    are cached on disk so a given tracking link is only fetched once across
   237	    runs. Failures fall back to the original URL silently.
   238	    """
   239	    if not urls:
   240	        return urls
   241	
   242	    cache = _load_cache()
   243	    to_fetch: list[tuple[int, str]] = []
   244	    resolved: list[str] = list(urls)
   245	
   246	    for i, url in enumerate(urls):
   247	        if not is_redirector(url):
   248	            continue
   249	        cached = cache.get(url)
   250	        if cached:
   251	            resolved[i] = cached
   252	            continue
   253	        to_fetch.append((i, url))
   254	
   255	    if to_fetch:
   256	        transient = 0
   257	        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=MAX_WORKERS) as pool:
   258	            futures = {pool.submit(_resolve_one, url): (i, url) for i, url in to_fetch}
   259	            for fut in as_completed(futures):
   260	                i, original = futures[fut]
   261	                final = fut.result()
   262	                if final is TRANSIENT_FAILURE:
   263	                    # Transient transport blip (timeout / DNS / reset). Keep the
   264	                    # original URL in the output so the pipeline never breaks,
   265	                    # but do NOT cache it — caching a transient failure as a
   266	                    # permanent no-op would suppress every future retry of a
   267	                    # redirector that is merely temporarily unreachable.
   268	                    resolved[i] = original
   269	                    transient += 1
   270	                    continue
   271	                resolved[i] = final
   272	                # Cache regardless of whether resolution actually changed the URL —
   273	                # caching the no-op saves us from re-trying broken redirectors.
   274	                # Empty string = dead-end (cached too, so we don't retry).
   275	                cache[original] = final
   276	        _save_cache(cache)
   277	        changed = sum(
   278	            1 for _, original in to_fetch
   279	            if cache.get(original) and cache.get(original) != original
   280	        )
   281	        dead = sum(1 for _, original in to_fetch if cache.get(original) == "")
   282	        print(
   283	            f"url_resolver: resolved {changed}/{len(to_fetch)} redirector URLs"
   284	            + (f" ({dead} dead-end dropped)" if dead else "")
   285	            + (f" ({transient} transient failures — not cached, will retry)" if transient else "")
   286	        )
   287	
   288	    # Drop dead-end URLs (empty strings) from the final list.
   289	    return [u for u in resolved if u]

   271	        "name": "Less Wrong",
   272	        "category": "broad_curious",
   273	        "priority": "normal",
   274	        "max_items": 5,
   275	    },
   276	    {
   277	        "url": "https://mathbabe.org/feed/",

   279	        "category": "broad_curious",
   280	        "priority": "normal",
   281	        "max_items": 3,
   282	    },
   283	]

   285	STRIP_HTML_RE = re.compile(r"<[^>]+>")
   286	MAX_TOTAL_ITEMS = 60
   287	
   288	
   289	def _entry_published_time(entry) -> datetime | None:
   290	    """Extract a timezone-aware datetime from a feed entry."""
   291	    for attr in ("published_parsed", "updated_parsed"):
   292	        parsed = getattr(entry, attr, None)
   293	        if parsed:
   294	            try:
   295	                return datetime(*parsed[:6], tzinfo=timezone.utc)
   296	            except (TypeError, ValueError):
   297	                continue
   298	    return None
   299	
   300	
   301	def _clean_summary(raw: str, limit: int = 300) -> str:
   302	    text = STRIP_HTML_RE.sub("", raw or "")
   303	    text = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text).strip()
   304	    return text[:limit]
   305	
   306	
   307	def fetch_rss_feeds_with_health(hours_back: int = 26) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]:
   308	    """Fetch recent entries from all configured RSS feeds, plus a health dict.
   309	
   310	    Returns ``(items, health)`` where ``items`` matches tier2_scan()'s shape and
   311	    ``health`` is ``{"feeds_ok": int, "feeds_total": int}``. ``feeds_ok`` counts
   312	    feeds that fetched+parsed successfully **regardless of item count**, so the
   313	    caller can distinguish a genuine zero-item day (all feeds OK, nothing recent)
   314	    from a total outage (every feed errored) — the latter must not masquerade as
   315	    a quiet news day.
   316	    """
   317	    cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=hours_back)
   318	    seen_urls: set[str] = set()
   319	    items: list[dict] = []
   320	    feeds_ok = 0
   321	    feeds_total = len(RSS_FEEDS)
   322	
   323	    for feed_config in RSS_FEEDS:
   324	        feed_url = feed_config["url"]
   325	        feed_name = feed_config["name"]
   326	        max_items = feed_config.get("max_items", 10)
   327	
   328	        try:
   329	            parsed = feedparser.parse(feed_url)
   330	        except Exception as exc:
   331	            print(f"  RSS fetch failed for {feed_name}: {exc}")
   332	            continue
   333	
   334	        if parsed.bozo and not parsed.entries:
   335	            print(f"  RSS parse error for {feed_name}: {parsed.bozo_exception}")
   336	            continue
   337	
   338	        # Feed fetched + parsed cleanly (may still legitimately have 0 recent
   339	        # items). Count it as healthy so an all-zero day with every feed OK
   340	        # reads as "quiet", not "outage".
   341	        feeds_ok += 1
   342	        count = 0
   343	        for entry in parsed.entries:
   344	            if count >= max_items:
   345	                break
   346	
   347	            link = (entry.get("link") or "").strip()
   348	            if not link or link in seen_urls:
   349	                continue
   350	
   351	            published = _entry_published_time(entry)
   352	            if published and published < cutoff:
   353	                continue
   354	
   355	            title = (entry.get("title") or "").strip()
   356	            if not title:
   357	                continue
   358	
   359	            seen_urls.add(link)
   360	            items.append({
   361	                "source_name": feed_name,
   362	                "subject": title,
   363	                "snippet": _clean_summary(entry.get("summary", "")),
   364	                "urls": [link],
   365	                "tier": 2,
   366	                "category": feed_config["category"],
   367	                "priority": feed_config["priority"],
   368	                "score": None,
   369	            })
   370	            count += 1
   371	
   372	        if count:
   373	            print(f"  {feed_name}: {count} items")
   374	
   375	    # Cap total to avoid flooding the selector prompt
   376	    if len(items) > MAX_TOTAL_ITEMS:
   377	        items = items[:MAX_TOTAL_ITEMS]
   378	
   379	    return items, {"feeds_ok": feeds_ok, "feeds_total": feeds_total}
   380	
   381	
   382	def fetch_rss_feeds(hours_back: int = 26) -> list[dict]:
   383	    """Backwards-compatible wrapper returning just the item list.
   384	
   385	    Prefer :func:`fetch_rss_feeds_with_health` when the caller needs to tell a
   386	    total feed outage apart from a genuine zero-item day.
   387	    """
   388	    items, _health = fetch_rss_feeds_with_health(hours_back=hours_back)
   389	    return items

    46	        timeout=15,

   108	            "url": entry.get("url", ""),
   109	            "local_path": "",
   110	            "note": entry.get("note", ""),
   111	            "score": score,
   112	            "content_status": "not_applicable",
   113	            "metadata": {
   114	                "article_id": entry.get("article_id"),
   115	                "article_source": entry.get("article_source"),
   116	                "slot": entry.get("slot"),
   117	                "digest_date": entry.get("date"),
   118	            },
   119	            "created_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"),
   120	        })
   121	    if taste_records:
   122	        bridged = append_taste_evidence(taste_records)
   123	        if bridged:
   124	            print(f"Bridged {bridged} feedback entries to taste evidence.")
   125	
   126	    print(f"Processed {processed} new feedback entries from {len(issues)} issue(s).")
   127	
   128	
   129	if __name__ == "__main__":
   130	    main()
     1	"""Harvest feedback from email replies to Daily Reads digests.
     2	
     3	Looks for replies to "Daily Reads" emails and parses simple feedback like:
     4	  slot 1: 3
     5	  slot 2: 1
     6	  1 3
     7	  3 1 too generic
     8	  slot 3: okay
     9	"""
    10	
    11	import json
    12	import os
    13	import re
    14	import sys
    15	from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
    16	
    17	from gmail_reader import get_gmail_service
    18	from project_data import append_taste_evidence, enrich_feedback_entry, evidence_id_for
    19	
    20	FEEDBACK_RE = re.compile(
    21	    r"^\s*(?:slot\s*)?(\d{1,2})[ \t]*[:\-]?[ \t]*(strong|great|good|fine|okay|ok|miss|bad|not useful|irrelevant|boring|1|2|3)(?:[ \t]*[,\-]?[ \t]*([^\n]+))?\s*$",
    22	    re.IGNORECASE | re.MULTILINE,
    23	)
    24	
    25	SCORE_MAP = {
    26	    "strong": 3,
    27	    "great": 3,
    28	    "good": 3,
    29	    "3": 3,
    30	    "fine": 2,
    31	    "okay": 2,
    32	    "ok": 2,
    33	    "2": 2,
    34	    "miss": 1,
    35	    "bad": 1,
    36	    "not useful": 1,
    37	    "irrelevant": 1,
    38	    "boring": 1,
    39	    "1": 1,
    40	}
    41	
    42	
    43	def load_json(path, default=None):
    44	    try:
    45	        with open(path, "r") as f:
    46	            return json.load(f)
    47	    except (FileNotFoundError, json.JSONDecodeError):
    48	        return default if default is not None else []
    49	
    50	
    51	def save_json(path, data):
    52	    with open(path, "w") as f:
    53	        json.dump(data, f, indent=2)
    54	
    55	
    56	def extract_reply_text(payload):
    57	    """Extract the reply portion of an email, stripping quoted content."""
    58	    import base64
    59	
    60	    text = ""
    61	    if payload.get("mimeType") == "text/plain":
    62	        data = payload.get("body", {}).get("data", "")
    63	        if data:
    64	            text = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(data).decode("utf-8", errors="replace")
    65	    for part in payload.get("parts", []):
    66	        result = extract_reply_text(part)
    67	        if result:
    68	            text = result
    69	            break
    70	
    71	    if not text:
    72	        return ""
    73	
    74	    # Strip quoted reply (lines starting with > or "On ... wrote:")
    75	    lines = []
    76	    for line in text.split("\n"):
    77	        if re.match(r"^On .+ wrote:", line):
    78	            break
    79	        if line.strip().startswith(">"):
    80	            break
    81	        lines.append(line)
    82	    return "\n".join(lines).strip()
    83	
    84	
    85	def main():
    86	    print("=" * 60)
    87	    print("  EMAIL FEEDBACK CHECK")
    88	    print("=" * 60)
    89	
    90	    try:
    91	        service = get_gmail_service()
    92	    except Exception as e:
    93	        print(f"Gmail auth failed: {e}")
    94	        return
    95	
    96	    # Search for feedback messages in the last 48 hours. This covers two flows:
    97	    #   1. Manual replies to the digest email (subject "Re: 📰 Daily Reads — DATE")
    98	    #   2. Slack feedback buttons, which open a fresh mailto: composition with
    99	    #      subject "Daily Reads feedback DATE" — NOT a threaded reply, so we
   100	    #      cannot use `is:reply` here or those messages get filtered out.
   101	    cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=48)
   102	    date_query = cutoff.strftime("%Y/%m/%d")
   103	    query = f'after:{date_query} subject:"Daily Reads" in:inbox'
   104	
   105	    try:
   106	        resp = service.users().messages().list(
   107	            userId="me", q=query, maxResults=20
   108	        ).execute()
   109	    except Exception as e:
   110	        print(f"Gmail search failed: {e}")
   111	        return
   112	
   113	    messages = resp.get("messages", [])
   114	    if not messages:
   115	        print("No email feedback replies found.")
   116	        return
   117	
   118	    feedback = load_json("feedback_log.json", [])
   119	    existing = {(e["date"], e["slot"], e.get("source", "")) for e in feedback}
   120	    processed = 0
   121	
   122	    for msg_stub in messages:
   123	        msg = service.users().messages().get(
   124	            userId="me", id=msg_stub["id"], format="full"
   125	        ).execute()
   126	
   127	        headers = {h["name"].lower(): h["value"] for h in msg["payload"]["headers"]}
   128	        subject = headers.get("subject", "")
   129	
   130	        # Extract date from subject like "Re: Daily Reads — 2026-03-25"
   131	        date_match = re.search(r"(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})", subject)
   132	        if not date_match:
   133	            continue
   134	        digest_date = date_match.group(1)
   135	
   136	        # Get the reply text
   137	        reply_text = extract_reply_text(msg["payload"])
   138	        if not reply_text:
   139	            continue
   140	
   141	        # Parse feedback lines
   142	        for match in FEEDBACK_RE.finditer(reply_text):
   143	            slot = int(match.group(1))
   144	            rating = match.group(2).lower()
   145	            note = (match.group(3) or "").strip()
   146	            score = SCORE_MAP.get(rating, 3)
   147	
   148	            if slot < 1 or slot > 14:
   149	                continue
   150	
   151	            key = (digest_date, slot, "email_reply")
   152	            if key in existing:
   153	                print(f"  Duplicate: {digest_date} slot{slot} — skipping")
   154	                continue
   155	
   156	            entry = enrich_feedback_entry(digest_date, slot, "email_reply", score, note)
   157	            entry["source"] = "email_reply"
   158	            feedback.append(entry)
   159	            existing.add(key)
   160	            processed += 1
   161	            print(f"  Recorded: {digest_date} slot{slot} score{score} — {note or '(no note)'}")
   162	
   163	    save_json("feedback_log.json", feedback)
   164	
   165	    # Bridge score-1 and score-3 entries into taste evidence
   166	    taste_records = []
   167	    for entry in feedback:
   168	        score = entry.get("score")
   169	        if score not in (1, 3):
   170	            continue
   171	        taste_records.append({
   172	            "id": evidence_id_for(f"feedback|{entry.get('date')}|{entry.get('slot')}|{entry.get('channel', '')}"),
   173	            "kind": f"daily_rating_{score}",
   174	            "source_channel": "daily_scoring",
   175	            "title": entry.get("headline", ""),
   176	            "url": entry.get("url", ""),
   177	            "local_path": "",
   178	            "note": entry.get("note", ""),
   179	            "score": score,
   180	            "content_status": "not_applicable",
   181	            "metadata": {
   182	                "article_id": entry.get("article_id"),
   183	                "article_source": entry.get("article_source"),
   184	                "slot": entry.get("slot"),
   185	                "digest_date": entry.get("date"),
   186	            },
   187	            "created_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"),
   188	        })
   189	    if taste_records:
   190	        bridged = append_taste_evidence(taste_records)
   191	        if bridged:
   192	            print(f"Bridged {bridged} feedback entries to taste evidence.")
   193	
   194	    print(f"Processed {processed} feedback entries from {len(messages)} reply email(s).")
   195	
   196	
   197	if __name__ == "__main__":
   198	    main()
     1	"""Harvest external taste exemplars from Gmail alias and label intake paths."""
     2	
     3	import base64
     4	import os
     5	import re
     6	from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
     7	from email.utils import parseaddr
     8	
     9	from gmail_reader import clean_url, get_gmail_service, is_probable_article_url
    10	from project_data import evidence_id_for, load_json, save_json, taste_evidence_path
    11	
    12	EXEMPLAR_PATH = taste_evidence_path()
    13	URL_RE = re.compile(r"https?://\S+", re.IGNORECASE)
    14	ATTACHMENT_TYPES = {".pdf", ".txt", ".md", ".html", ".htm", ".docx", ".doc"}
    15	
    16	
    17	def configured_taste_alias() -> str:
    18	    return os.environ.get("TASTE_EMAIL_ALIAS") or "jroypeterson+taste@gmail.com"
    19	
    20	
    21	def configured_taste_label() -> str:
    22	    return os.environ.get("TASTE_GMAIL_LABEL") or "taste"
    23	
    24	
    25	def header_map(payload: dict) -> dict:
    26	    return {
    27	        header["name"].lower(): header["value"]
    28	        for header in payload.get("headers", [])
    29	        if isinstance(header, dict) and header.get("name")
    30	    }
    31	
    32	
    33	def decode_body_data(data: str) -> str:
    34	    if not data:
    35	        return ""
    36	    return base64.urlsafe_b64decode(data).decode("utf-8", errors="replace")
    37	
    38	
    39	def extract_text_bodies(payload: dict) -> list[str]:
    40	    bodies = []
    41	    mime_type = payload.get("mimeType")
    42	    if mime_type == "text/plain":
    43	        bodies.append(decode_body_data(payload.get("body", {}).get("data", "")))
    44	    for part in payload.get("parts", []):
    45	        bodies.extend(extract_text_bodies(part))
    46	    return bodies
    47	
    48	
    49	def extract_attachment_parts(payload: dict) -> list[dict]:
    50	    attachments = []
    51	    filename = (payload.get("filename") or "").strip()
    52	    body = payload.get("body", {})
    53	    attachment_id = body.get("attachmentId")
    54	    if filename and attachment_id:
    55	        attachments.append(
    56	            {
    57	                "filename": filename,
    58	                "mime_type": payload.get("mimeType", ""),
    59	                "attachment_id": attachment_id,
    60	            }
    61	        )
    62	    for part in payload.get("parts", []):
    63	        attachments.extend(extract_attachment_parts(part))
    64	    return attachments
    65	
    66	
    67	def extract_candidate_urls(text: str) -> list[str]:
    68	    urls = []
    69	    for match in URL_RE.findall(text or ""):
    70	        cleaned = clean_url(match.rstrip(").,>"))
    71	        if is_probable_article_url(cleaned) and cleaned not in urls:
    72	            urls.append(cleaned)
    73	    return urls
    74	
    75	
    76	def extract_note(text: str) -> str:
    77	    text = (text or "").strip()
    78	    if not text:
    79	        return ""
    80	    explicit = re.search(r"why i liked it:\s*(.+)", text, re.IGNORECASE | re.DOTALL)
    81	    if explicit:
    82	        return explicit.group(1).strip()[:500]
    83	    redacted = URL_RE.sub("", text)
    84	    redacted = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", redacted).strip(" -:\n\t")
    85	    return redacted[:500]
    86	
    87	
    88	def message_query(hours_back: int) -> str:
    89	    cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=hours_back)
    90	    date_query = cutoff.strftime("%Y/%m/%d")
    91	    return f"after:{date_query} (to:{configured_taste_alias()} OR label:{configured_taste_label()})"
    92	
    93	
    94	def build_url_exemplar(message: dict, headers: dict, url: str, note: str) -> dict:
    95	    subject = headers.get("subject", "(no subject)")
    96	    sender_raw = headers.get("from", "")
    97	    _, sender_email = parseaddr(sender_raw)
    98	    message_id = message.get("id", "")
    99	    return {
   100	        "id": evidence_id_for(f"email-url|{message_id}|{url}"),
   101	        "kind": "positive_exemplar",
   102	        "source_channel": "email",
   103	        "title": subject,
   104	        "url": url,
   105	        "local_path": "",
   106	        "note": note,
   107	        "score": None,
   108	        "content_status": "unfetched",
   109	        "metadata": {
   110	            "message_id": message_id,
   111	            "subject": subject,
   112	            "sender": sender_raw,
   113	            "sender_email": sender_email.lower(),
   114	            "label_ids": message.get("labelIds", []),
   115	        },
   116	        "created_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"),
   117	    }
   118	
   119	
   120	def build_attachment_exemplar(message: dict, headers: dict, attachment: dict, note: str) -> dict:
   121	    subject = headers.get("subject", "(no subject)")
   122	    sender_raw = headers.get("from", "")
   123	    _, sender_email = parseaddr(sender_raw)
   124	    message_id = message.get("id", "")
   125	    filename = attachment.get("filename", "")
   126	    return {
   127	        "id": evidence_id_for(f"email-attachment|{message_id}|{filename}"),
   128	        "kind": "positive_exemplar",
   129	        "source_channel": "email",
   130	        "title": subject or filename or "(attachment)",
   131	        "url": "",
   132	        "local_path": "",
   133	        "note": note,
   134	        "score": None,
   135	        "content_status": "email_attachment_pending",
   136	        "metadata": {
   137	            "message_id": message_id,
   138	            "subject": subject,
   139	            "sender": sender_raw,
   140	            "sender_email": sender_email.lower(),
   141	            "label_ids": message.get("labelIds", []),
   142	            "filename": filename,
   143	            "mime_type": attachment.get("mime_type", ""),
   144	            "attachment_id": attachment.get("attachment_id", ""),
   145	        },
   146	        "created_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"),
   147	    }
   148	
   149	
   150	def harvest_messages(service, hours_back: int = 24 * 14) -> list[dict]:
   151	    query = message_query(hours_back)
   152	    response = service.users().messages().list(userId="me", q=query, maxResults=100).execute()
   153	    return response.get("messages", [])
   154	
   155	
   156	def main():
   157	    print("=" * 60)
   158	    print("  EMAIL EXEMPLAR CHECK")
   159	    print("=" * 60)
   160	
   161	    try:
   162	        service = get_gmail_service()
   163	    except Exception as exc:
   164	        print(f"Gmail auth failed: {exc}")
   165	        return
   166	
   167	    messages = harvest_messages(service)
   168	    if not messages:
   169	        print("No exemplar emails found.")
   170	        return
   171	
   172	    exemplars = load_json(EXEMPLAR_PATH, [])
   173	    existing_ids = {entry.get("id") for entry in exemplars if isinstance(entry, dict)}
   174	    processed = 0
   175	
   176	    for message_stub in messages:
   177	        message = service.users().messages().get(
   178	            userId="me", id=message_stub["id"], format="full"
   179	        ).execute()
   180	        headers = header_map(message.get("payload", {}))
   181	        text = "\n\n".join(part for part in extract_text_bodies(message.get("payload", {})) if part)
   182	        text = text or message.get("snippet", "")
   183	        urls = extract_candidate_urls(text)
   184	        note = extract_note(text)
   185	
   186	        for url in urls:
   187	            exemplar = build_url_exemplar(message, headers, url, note)
   188	            if exemplar["id"] in existing_ids:
   189	                continue
   190	            exemplars.append(exemplar)
   191	            existing_ids.add(exemplar["id"])
   192	            processed += 1
   193	            print(f"  Recorded URL exemplar: {url}")
   194	
   195	        for attachment in extract_attachment_parts(message.get("payload", {})):
   196	            extension = os.path.splitext(attachment.get("filename", ""))[1].lower()
   197	            if extension and extension not in ATTACHMENT_TYPES:
   198	                continue
   199	            exemplar = build_attachment_exemplar(message, headers, attachment, note)
   200	            if exemplar["id"] in existing_ids:
   201	                continue
   202	            exemplars.append(exemplar)
   203	            existing_ids.add(exemplar["id"])
   204	            processed += 1
   205	            print(f"  Recorded attachment exemplar: {attachment.get('filename', '(unnamed)')}")
   206	
   207	    save_json(EXEMPLAR_PATH, exemplars)
   208	    print(f"Processed {processed} exemplar entries from {len(messages)} email(s).")
   209	
   210	
   211	if __name__ == "__main__":
   212	    main()
     1	"""Process accept/reject/modify decisions for staged criteria proposals."""
     2	
     3	import os
     4	import re
     5	import subprocess
     6	from datetime import datetime, timezone
     7	
     8	import requests
     9	
    10	from main import (
    11	    CRITERIA_STATE_PATH,
    12	    PROPOSED_CRITERIA_PATH,
    13	    REPO,
    14	    load_criteria_state,
    15	    save_criteria_state,
    16	)
    17	
    18	API = "https://api.github.com"
    19	TITLE_RE = re.compile(r"Criteria Update:\s*(accept|reject|modify)\s+(.+)", re.IGNORECASE)
    20	
    21	# Durable state touched by a criteria decision. Committed to git BEFORE the
    22	# GitHub issue is closed so a crash between the two can't leave the issue
    23	# closed (durable on GitHub) while the state change is lost on the ephemeral
    24	# runner — which would silently drop the decision forever.
    25	CRITERIA_DURABLE_PATHS = [
    26	    CRITERIA_STATE_PATH,
    27	    "selection_criteria.md",
    28	    PROPOSED_CRITERIA_PATH,
    29	]
    30	
    31	
    32	def commit_durable_state(paths, message: str) -> bool:
    33	    """Git add + commit + push the given state files. Returns True if the state
    34	    is durably persisted (committed, or nothing to commit), False on any git
    35	    failure — in which case the caller must NOT close the issue so the decision
    36	    is retried on the next run instead of being lost."""
    37	    existing = [p for p in paths if os.path.exists(p)]
    38	    if not existing:
    39	        return False
    40	    try:
    41	        subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.name", "daily-reads-bot"], check=True)
    42	        subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.email", "bot@daily-reads"], check=True)
    43	        subprocess.run(["git", "add", *existing], check=True)
    44	        # Nothing staged => already durable; treat as success.
    45	        if subprocess.run(["git", "diff", "--staged", "--quiet"]).returncode == 0:
    46	            return True
    47	        subprocess.run(["git", "commit", "-m", message], check=True)
    48	        subprocess.run(["git", "push"], check=True)
    49	        return True
    50	    except Exception as exc:
    51	        print(f"  criteria: durable-state commit failed ({exc}) — leaving issue open to retry")
    52	        return False
    53	
    54	
    55	def append_history(state: dict, pending: dict, resolution: str, note: str = ""):
    56	    state.setdefault("history", []).append({
    57	        **pending,
    58	        "resolved_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"),
    59	        "resolution": resolution,
    60	        "resolution_note": note,
    61	    })
    62	
    63	
    64	def apply_proposal():
    65	    with open(PROPOSED_CRITERIA_PATH, "r") as f:
    66	        proposed = f.read()
    67	    with open("selection_criteria.md", "w") as f:
    68	        f.write(proposed)
    69	
    70	
    71	def main():
    72	    token = os.environ.get("GITHUB_TOKEN")
    73	    if not token:
    74	        print("No GITHUB_TOKEN set — skipping criteria decision processing")
    75	        return
    76	
    77	    state = load_criteria_state()
    78	    pending = state.get("pending")
    79	    if not pending:
    80	        print("No pending criteria proposal.")
    81	        return
    82	
    83	    headers = {
    84	        "Authorization": f"token {token}",
    85	        "Accept": "application/vnd.github+json",
    86	    }
    87	
    88	    resp = requests.get(
    89	        f"{API}/repos/{REPO}/issues",
    90	        headers=headers,
    91	        params={"labels": "criteria-update", "state": "open", "per_page": 100},
    92	        timeout=15,
    93	    )
    94	    resp.raise_for_status()
    95	    issues = resp.json()
    96	    if not issues:
    97	        print("No criteria decision issues to process.")
    98	        return
    99	
   100	    processed = 0
   101	    for issue in issues:
   102	        match = TITLE_RE.search(issue.get("title", ""))
   103	        if not match:
   104	            continue
   105	
   106	        action = match.group(1).lower()
   107	        proposal_id = match.group(2).strip()
   108	        if proposal_id != pending.get("proposal_id"):
   109	            print(f"  Skipping issue #{issue['number']}: not for current proposal")
   110	            continue
   111	
   112	        body = issue.get("body") or ""
   113	        note = ""
   114	        note_match = re.search(r"Requested changes:\s*(.+)", body, re.IGNORECASE | re.DOTALL)
   115	        if note_match:
   116	            note = note_match.group(1).strip()
   117	
   118	        if action == "accept":
   119	            apply_proposal()
   120	            append_history(state, pending, "accepted")
   121	            state["pending"] = None
   122	            print(f"  Accepted proposal {proposal_id}")
   123	        elif action == "reject":
   124	            append_history(state, pending, "rejected")
   125	            state["pending"] = None
   126	            print(f"  Rejected proposal {proposal_id}")
   127	        elif action == "modify":
   128	            pending["status"] = "modification_requested"
   129	            pending["modification_note"] = note
   130	            pending["requested_at"] = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
   131	            state["pending"] = pending
   132	            print(f"  Modification requested for {proposal_id}")
   133	
   134	        save_criteria_state(state)
   135	
   136	        # Commit the durable state to git BEFORE closing the issue. If the
   137	        # commit fails, leave the issue OPEN so the decision is reprocessed
   138	        # next run rather than lost (issue closed on GitHub, state discarded
   139	        # on the ephemeral runner).
   140	        if not commit_durable_state(
   141	            CRITERIA_DURABLE_PATHS, f"criteria: {action} {proposal_id}"
   142	        ):
   143	            print(f"  Not closing issue #{issue['number']} — state not durably committed")
   144	            continue
   145	
   146	        requests.patch(
   147	            f"{API}/repos/{REPO}/issues/{issue['number']}",
   148	            headers=headers,
   149	            json={"state": "closed"},
   150	            timeout=10,
   151	        )
   152	        processed += 1
   153	
   154	    print(f"Processed {processed} criteria decision issue(s).")
   155	
   156	
   157	if __name__ == "__main__":
   158	    main()

     2	
     3	import os

     8	import anthropic

    10	from project_data import append_taste_evidence, evidence_id_for, load_taste_evidence
    11	from preference_learning import update_learned_preferences
    12	
    13	REPO = "jroypeterson/daily-reads"
    14	API = "https://api.github.com"
    15	TITLE_RE = re.compile(r"Taste:\s*(.+)", re.IGNORECASE)
    16	URL_RE = re.compile(r"https?://\S+")
    17	
    18
