     1	"""Slack Block Kit ceiling guard — VENDORED, deliberately, not a shim to `_shared/`.
     2	
     3	This project's digest posts from **GitHub Actions** (`.github/workflows/daily.yml`),
     4	and `<workspace>/_shared/` is a Dropbox sibling that `actions/checkout@v5` does not
     5	fetch. The previous `slack_blocks_client.py` shim inserted `_shared/slack_blocks` on
     6	`sys.path` and imported from there — which works on JP's laptop and **fails on every
     7	CI run**, where `_get()` returned None, `problems()` returned `[]`, and the payload
     8	went out unchunked. The guard was inert in the only place this lane actually posts on
     9	schedule, while the fleet docs recorded daily-reads as covered.
    10	
    11	That is the failure this fleet has already paid for once, and the reason the standing
    12	rule is *vendor, don't shim, for CI projects*. `forensic_triage/block_ceiling.py` is
    13	the same decision for the same reason.
    14	
    15	WHY THIS LANE NEEDS IT. `main.py` already handles Slack's 3000-chars-per-section limit
    16	via `_split_oversized_section_blocks`, but nothing checked the **50-blocks-per-message**
    17	ceiling — a different limit behind the same opaque `invalid_blocks` rejection. The
    18	digest's block count scales with how many articles were found, so the payload is
    19	largest exactly on the days worth reading.
    20	
    21	Canonical implementation + tests: `<workspace>/_shared/slack_blocks/`. If the ceilings
    22	change, fix that copy first; this one is a follower.
    23	"""
    24	from __future__ import annotations
    25	
    26	__all__ = [
    27	    "MAX_BLOCKS",
    28	    "MAX_SECTION_CHARS",
    29	    "MAX_HEADER_CHARS",
    30	    "MAX_CONTEXT_ELEMENTS",
    31	    "MAX_TEXT_CHARS",
    32	    "validate_blocks",
    33	    "chunk_blocks",
    34	    "render_blocks_to_text",
    35	    "safe_payloads",
    36	]
    37	
    38	__version__ = "1.0.0"
    39	
    40	MAX_BLOCKS = 50           # blocks per message — the one that actually bit us
    41	MAX_SECTION_CHARS = 3000  # section text, and each context element
    42	MAX_HEADER_CHARS = 150    # header text
    43	MAX_CONTEXT_ELEMENTS = 10  # elements[] per context block
    44	MAX_TEXT_CHARS = 40000    # top-level `text` (the plain-text fallback)

    47	def validate_blocks(blocks: list[dict]) -> list[str]:
    48	    """Return every Block Kit limit this payload breaks. Empty list == valid.
    49	
    50	    Checks the count ceiling first because that is the one that is invisible in any
    51	    single block: every block can be individually legal and the message still rejected.
    52	    """
    53	    problems: list[str] = []

    55	    if not isinstance(blocks, list):
    56	        return [f"payload is {type(blocks).__name__}, expected a list of blocks"]
    57	    if not blocks:

    60	        problems.append(
    61	            f"{len(blocks)} blocks exceeds Slack's limit of {MAX_BLOCKS} per message")
    62

    65	            problems.append(f"block {i}: {type(b).__name__}, expected a dict")
    66	            continue

   120	def chunk_blocks(blocks: list[dict], *, max_blocks: int = MAX_BLOCKS) -> list[list[dict]]:
   121	    """Split into messages of at most `max_blocks`, preferring a divider as the seam.
   122	
   123	    Splitting rather than truncating is the whole point. Backing up to the last divider
   124	    in the window keeps a table from being cut mid-way; the search floor is the halfway
   125	    point so a divider-sparse payload still makes progress instead of emitting many tiny
   126	    chunks (or, worse, looping forever on a zero-length advance).
   127	    """
   128	    if max_blocks < 1:
   129	        raise ValueError("max_blocks must be >= 1")
   130	    if len(blocks) <= max_blocks:
   131	        return [list(blocks)]
   132	
   133	    out: list[list[dict]] = []
   134	    i = 0
   135	    n = len(blocks)
   136	    while i < n:
   137	        end = min(i + max_blocks, n)
   138	        if end < n:
   139	            floor = i + max_blocks // 2
   140	            seam = next((j for j in range(end - 1, floor, -1)
   141	                         if isinstance(blocks[j], dict)
   142	                         and blocks[j].get("type") == "divider"), None)
   143	            if seam is not None:
   144	                end = seam + 1
   145	        out.append(blocks[i:end])
   146	        i = end
   147	    return out
   148	
   149	
   150	def render_blocks_to_text(blocks: list[dict]) -> str:
   151	    """Flatten Block Kit to plain markdown-ish text for the last-resort fallback.
   152	
   153	    Sections keep their mrkdwn (code fences survive), dividers become a rule, context
   154	    elements become quote lines, headers become bold lines.
   155	    """
   156	    parts: list[str] = []
   157	    for b in blocks:
   158	        if not isinstance(b, dict):
   159	            continue
   160	        btype = b.get("type")
   161	        if btype == "divider":
   162	            parts.append("---")
   163	        elif btype == "header":
   164	            t = (b.get("text") or {}).get("text", "")
   165	            if t:
   166	                parts.append(f"*{t}*")
   167	        elif btype == "section":
   168	            t = (b.get("text") or {}).get("text", "")
   169	            if t:
   170	                parts.append(t)
   171	            for fl in b.get("fields") or []:
   172	                ft = (fl or {}).get("text", "")
   173	                if ft:
   174	                    parts.append(ft)
   175	        elif btype == "context":
   176	            for el in b.get("elements") or []:
   177	                t = (el or {}).get("text", "")
   178	                if t:
   179	                    parts.append("> " + t)
   180	    return "\n\n".join(p for p in parts if p) + "\n"
   181	
   182	
   183	def safe_payloads(blocks: list[dict], fallback_text: str,
   184	                  *, max_blocks: int = MAX_BLOCKS) -> list[dict]:
   185	    """Ready-to-POST payload(s) for `blocks`, split if needed.
   186	
   187	    Every payload carries a `text` — Slack uses it for notifications and accessibility,
   188	    and it is what survives if the blocks are rejected. Continuation messages are
   189	    labelled so a reader can tell a 3-part digest from three unrelated posts.
   190	    """
   191	    chunks = chunk_blocks(blocks, max_blocks=max_blocks)
   192	    total = len(chunks)
   193	    payloads = []
   194	    for n, chunk in enumerate(chunks, 1):
   195	        # The FIRST message is not a continuation of anything — labelling it "cont. 1/3"
   196	        # reads as a missing part 0 and makes the reader hunt for a post that never existed.
   197	        text = fallback_text if n == 1 else f"{fallback_text} (cont. {n}/{total})"
   198	        payloads.append({"blocks": chunk, "text": text[:MAX_TEXT_CHARS]})
   199	    return payloads

CLAUDE.md:47:`deliver_reader` in `main.py` pushes the day's **top picks** (`articles`) + **always-read** items into Readwise Reader so they're queued to read in the app. Uses the Readwise REST API (`POST https://readwise.io/api/v3/save/`), NOT the interactive MCP — the cron can't reach interactive MCP servers. Auth is the static personal token in `READWISE_TOKEN` (header `Authorization: Token <token>`), set as a GH Actions secret and kept locally in `daily-reads/.env` (gitignored). Mint/rotate at https://readwise.io/access_token.
CONTEXT.md:88:- the task helper runs native Windows Python (not WSL), registers a daily time trigger plus an at-logon fallback, with catch-up-after-missed-run behavior enabled
.github/workflows/daily.yml:4:  schedule:
.github/workflows/daily.yml:5:    - cron: '0 12 * * *'  # 7am ET (noon UTC)
.github/workflows/daily.yml:6:  workflow_dispatch:  # manual trigger

codex
### 1. Minimum-slot validation is dead, so severely incomplete digests report success

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `main.py:975`, `main.py:1784`, `main.py:2921`
- **Claim**: The required-slot validator is never called, and `verify_shortlist` returns even one or two articles as a successful digest.
- **Failure scenario**: Only one candidate passes verification; the run publishes that single article to Slack, Pages, and Reader and posts an `ok` heartbeat instead of retrying or failing.
- **Evidence**: `validate_selected_articles` requires slots 1–3 at lines 977–1048, but has no production caller. Lines 1784–1787 return `verified` identically whether its length is at least three or not, while lines 2922–2925 retry only an empty list. The committed artifacts show this is active: 23 of the 30 runs since 2026-09-01 delivered fewer than three articles, including one article on 2026-09-17. Regression class: **a-check-that-silently-matches-nothing**.

### 2. Delivered-article identity splits one URL into different IDs, allowing repeated publication

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `main.py:307`, `main.py:1305`, `main.py:2841`; `project_data.py:23`
- **Claim**: When Claude removes or changes tracking parameters, the recorded delivered ID no longer matches the candidate ID used by the next run’s exclusion filter.
- **Failure scenario**: A McKinsey candidate URL contains `?__dU__=...&__F__=...`, but Claude returns the same URL without that query; the first run records the canonical-URL fallback ID, the next run compares against the query-bearing candidate ID, and republishes the same article.
- **Evidence**: Candidate IDs hash `primary_url` at lines 307–315; `normalize_url` preserves the full query at `project_data.py:23–33`; exclusion compares those candidate IDs at lines 1309–1318. When delivered URL lookup misses, lines 2866–2868 record a different fallback ID. This occurred on 2026-09-28/29: “Novartis’s Christian Diehl on scaling AI beyond the demo” was published twice; ten distinct URLs were republished during September.

### 3. Dead-link substitution bypasses article verification

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `main.py:786`
- **Claim**: A broken verified top pick can be replaced after verification with any live triage candidate, without checking its substance or topical slot.
- **Failure scenario**: Slot 1’s verified healthcare article is dead; the highest-scoring eligible triage item is a finance or tech newsletter candidate, so it is published as Slot 1 using only its snippet despite never passing the article-body verifier.
- **Evidence**: Lines 795–803 pool all live triage candidates without a category constraint; lines 832–855 directly construct the replacement with the original slot and snippet. Verification already happened at line 1423, and no verifier is called afterward. The persisted URL log records this path firing on 2026-08-05. Regression class: **reviewed-thing-is-not-the-published-thing**.

### 4. Same-day reruns can attribute feedback to the wrong article

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `.github/workflows/daily.yml:6`; `main.py:61`, `main.py:2691`; `project_data.py:286`
- **Claim**: Feedback and artifacts are keyed only by UTC date and slot, so a manual rerun overwrites the mapping for the earlier published digest.
- **Failure scenario**: The scheduled run publishes article A in slot 1; a manual dispatch later that day publishes article B and overwrites `artifacts/runs/YYYY-MM-DD.json`; rating the still-visible first Slack post afterward trains the system as though B received A’s score.
- **Evidence**: Rating links contain only date and slot at lines 61–78. `save_run_artifact` writes one date-keyed file at lines 2691–2715, and `enrich_feedback_entry` resolves feedback solely through that current date/slot artifact at `project_data.py:286–320`. Same-day reruns have actually occurred, including four runs on 2026-04-15 and two each on 2026-06-03 and 2026-06-18.

### 5. Preference synthesis requests evidence IDs that are never supplied

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `preference_learning.py:154`
- **Claim**: Full synthesis accepts fabricated evidence foreign keys and publishes their counts because the prompt demands record IDs but omits those IDs from the evidence text.
- **Failure scenario**: A new exemplar triggers synthesis; Claude sees its title and note but not its `ev_...` ID, invents an identifier, and the resulting preference is presented to the selector as supported by evidence that cannot be joined to any record.
- **Evidence**: `format_evidence_block` at lines 154–166 omits `e["id"]`; lines 201–205 nevertheless require exact evidence IDs. Lines 232–243 accept the response without referential validation, while `main.py:228–229` and `preference_learning.py:292–293` publish the raw list length as “evidence points.” In the current committed state, 70 of 85 preference evidence references do not exist in `taste_evidence.json`.

### 6. Malformed JSON is converted to empty state and then destructively saved

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `project_data.py:10`, `project_data.py:18`, `project_data.py:62`; `process_exemplar_content.py:76`
- **Claim**: A truncated state file is silently treated as a valid empty store, after which scheduled writers overwrite the recoverable contents with that empty fallback.
- **Failure scenario**: An interrupted write leaves `delivered_state.json` malformed; the next digest loads an empty delivery/Reader ledger, republishes previously sent URLs, and saves a new ledger containing only that run. Likewise, malformed `taste_evidence.json` is loaded as `[]` and the scheduled exemplar-content task unconditionally writes `[]` back.
- **Evidence**: `load_json` catches `JSONDecodeError` and returns the caller’s default at lines 10–15; `save_json` truncates before writing at lines 18–20. `load_delivered_state` converts the fallback into empty ledgers at lines 62–87, and `main.py:2918–2993` subsequently uses and saves them. The Windows-scheduled `process_exemplar_content` path loads and unconditionally saves the full taste collection at lines 76–83.

### 7. External acknowledgements and delivery happen before the workflow’s only durable commit

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `.github/workflows/daily.yml:25`, `.github/workflows/daily.yml:99`, `.github/workflows/daily.yml:134`; `process_feedback.py:87`; `process_taste.py:75`
- **Claim**: A late workflow or push failure can permanently lose closed feedback/taste issues and delivery state after the digest has already posted an `ok` heartbeat.
- **Failure scenario**: A feedback issue is harvested and closed, the digest publishes and posts `ok`, then `git push` is rejected because the documented local writer advanced `main`; the ephemeral runner loses the feedback and delivered-state changes, the closed issue is never fetched again, and the next run can redeliver articles.
- **Evidence**: Feedback and taste issues are closed at `process_feedback.py:87–93` and `process_taste.py:75–81`. The health fallback runs immediately after `main.py` at workflow lines 99–120, but the sole bulk state commit/push is later at lines 134–144. A successful heartbeat sentinel therefore suppresses the fallback even when that final persistence step fails.

### 8. The Windows scheduled task reports success when commit or push fails

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `register_daily_dropbox_taste_task.ps1:27`
- **Claim**: Git add, commit, and push return codes are ignored, allowing newly archived taste exemplars to remain indefinitely absent from production while Task Scheduler records success.
- **Failure scenario**: Dropbox ingestion archives a submitted file and commits its evidence locally, but a transient push failure occurs; the Python action exits zero, and later runs see a clean working tree so lines 27–31 never retry the outstanding push unless another taste change happens.
- **Evidence**: The wrapper checks ingestion subprocesses at lines 22–25, but ignores all three git results at lines 29–31. `process_dropbox_exemplars.py:164–174` moves the source file into the excluded archive before saving state, so the original intake item is no longer rediscovered.

### 9. The scheduled source-audit “hard failure” cannot fire for stale or dead sources

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `.github/workflows/daily.yml:70`; `validate_source.py:286`
- **Claim**: The workflow invokes `--audit` without `--ci`, while stale/dead findings exit nonzero only in CI mode.
- **Failure scenario**: A configured daily source has been silent for weeks; the audit detects it, but the alert payload is rejected or transiently fails, after which the audit step remains green and the normal digest heartbeat reports no source-audit degradation.
- **Evidence**: Workflow line 77 runs `python validate_source.py --audit`; the only failure exit is guarded by `if ci_mode` at lines 286–287. `_send_slack_alert` at lines 154–157 also never checks HTTP status, so a non-2xx response is treated as success. This contradicts the workflow’s lines 70–75 claim that the audit is a hard gate. Regression class: **prose-outliving-the-artifact-it-describes**.

### 10. Weekly always-read coverage counts items that delivery explicitly dropped

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `weekly_report.py:301`; `main.py:2952`
- **Claim**: The weekly report treats every pre-validation always-read candidate as delivered, even when URL validation removed it from every published surface.
- **Failure scenario**: An always-read source has one candidate whose URL returns 404; validation removes it from Slack, Pages, and Reader, but Friday’s report counts that source as delivered for the day and suppresses the “missing” alarm.
- **Evidence**: `save_candidate_artifact` runs before URL validation at lines 2952–2960, while validation drops broken always-read entries at lines 908–912. The weekly report then adds every matching candidate to `names_for_day` at lines 319–326 and labels that set “delivered.” Regression class: **contaminated-value-in-a-shared-collection**.

Verdict: The production pipeline has multiple High-severity correctness and durability failures; its current “live and well-hardened” assessment is not supported.
tokens used
235,926
### 1. Minimum-slot validation is dead, so severely incomplete digests report success

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `main.py:975`, `main.py:1784`, `main.py:2921`
- **Claim**: The required-slot validator is never called, and `verify_shortlist` returns even one or two articles as a successful digest.
- **Failure scenario**: Only one candidate passes verification; the run publishes that single article to Slack, Pages, and Reader and posts an `ok` heartbeat instead of retrying or failing.
- **Evidence**: `validate_selected_articles` requires slots 1–3 at lines 977–1048, but has no production caller. Lines 1784–1787 return `verified` identically whether its length is at least three or not, while lines 2922–2925 retry only an empty list. The committed artifacts show this is active: 23 of the 30 runs since 2026-09-01 delivered fewer than three articles, including one article on 2026-09-17. Regression class: **a-check-that-silently-matches-nothing**.

### 2. Delivered-article identity splits one URL into different IDs, allowing repeated publication

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `main.py:307`, `main.py:1305`, `main.py:2841`; `project_data.py:23`
- **Claim**: When Claude removes or changes tracking parameters, the recorded delivered ID no longer matches the candidate ID used by the next run’s exclusion filter.
- **Failure scenario**: A McKinsey candidate URL contains `?__dU__=...&__F__=...`, but Claude returns the same URL without that query; the first run records the canonical-URL fallback ID, the next run compares against the query-bearing candidate ID, and republishes the same article.
- **Evidence**: Candidate IDs hash `primary_url` at lines 307–315; `normalize_url` preserves the full query at `project_data.py:23–33`; exclusion compares those candidate IDs at lines 1309–1318. When delivered URL lookup misses, lines 2866–2868 record a different fallback ID. This occurred on 2026-09-28/29: “Novartis’s Christian Diehl on scaling AI beyond the demo” was published twice; ten distinct URLs were republished during September.

### 3. Dead-link substitution bypasses article verification

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `main.py:786`
- **Claim**: A broken verified top pick can be replaced after verification with any live triage candidate, without checking its substance or topical slot.
- **Failure scenario**: Slot 1’s verified healthcare article is dead; the highest-scoring eligible triage item is a finance or tech newsletter candidate, so it is published as Slot 1 using only its snippet despite never passing the article-body verifier.
- **Evidence**: Lines 795–803 pool all live triage candidates without a category constraint; lines 832–855 directly construct the replacement with the original slot and snippet. Verification already happened at line 1423, and no verifier is called afterward. The persisted URL log records this path firing on 2026-08-05. Regression class: **reviewed-thing-is-not-the-published-thing**.

### 4. Same-day reruns can attribute feedback to the wrong article

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `.github/workflows/daily.yml:6`; `main.py:61`, `main.py:2691`; `project_data.py:286`
- **Claim**: Feedback and artifacts are keyed only by UTC date and slot, so a manual rerun overwrites the mapping for the earlier published digest.
- **Failure scenario**: The scheduled run publishes article A in slot 1; a manual dispatch later that day publishes article B and overwrites `artifacts/runs/YYYY-MM-DD.json`; rating the still-visible first Slack post afterward trains the system as though B received A’s score.
- **Evidence**: Rating links contain only date and slot at lines 61–78. `save_run_artifact` writes one date-keyed file at lines 2691–2715, and `enrich_feedback_entry` resolves feedback solely through that current date/slot artifact at `project_data.py:286–320`. Same-day reruns have actually occurred, including four runs on 2026-04-15 and two each on 2026-06-03 and 2026-06-18.

### 5. Preference synthesis requests evidence IDs that are never supplied

- **Severity**: High
- **Location**: `preference_learning.py:154`
- **Claim**: Full synthesis accepts fabricated evidence foreign keys and publishes their counts because the prompt demands record IDs but omits those IDs from the evidence text.
- **Failure scenario**: A new exemplar triggers synthesis; Claude sees its title and note but not its `ev_...` ID, invents an identifier, and the resulting preference is presented to the selector as supported by evidence that cannot be joined to any record.
- **Evidence**: `format_evidence_block` at lines 154–166 omits `e["id"]`; lines 201–205 nevertheless require exact evidence IDs. Lines 232–243 accept the response without referential validation, while `main.py:228–229` and `preference_learning.py:292–293` publish the raw list length as “evidence points.” In the current committed state, 70 of 85 preference evidence references do not exist in `taste_evidence.json`.
